# Critic-pass log

Per-slice record of which specialist critics ran, which were skipped,
and why. The CLAUDE.md tier table tells you WHEN to skip; this log
records WHAT was actually skipped per slice so a future audit can
trace coverage gaps.

Format:

| Slice | Tier | Specialists run | Specialists skipped (rationale) | Critical findings caught |
|---|---|---|---|---|

If a slice's tier table cell says "Technical + UI + UX + Graphical"
but only Technical + UI ran, the row records UX + Graphical as
skipped with the reason. Empty "Specialists skipped" is the norm —
fill the column only when a deviation happened.

## Why this log exists

We adopted the tier-by-risk specialist matrix in commit `1908613b`
(CLAUDE.md cadence reform) after observing that slice 70-C's
mechanical ZoneCounter→ZoneIcon rename ran a full 3-specialist
pass for a low-risk slice. The tier system reduces over-application;
this log catches under-application — slices that should have run
more critics than they did.

It's also a forward-reference for new contributors: "did slice 70-X
get UX coverage?" is answerable by scanning one column.

| fix/variant-tabletop-card-speck-sizing (H2, 2026-05-04) | Trivial (TabletopBuckets.tsx — apply explicit `style={{ width: 'var(--card-size-medium, 80px)', height: 'calc(var(--card-size-medium, 80px) * 7 / 5)', flexShrink: 0 }}` on the per-card motion.div wrapper) | Builder | All critics skipped — sizing-bug fix surfaced by user on first live-game test ("the land I cast on my first turn is a speck on my screen, I can barely see it"). Root cause: CardFace's `battlefield` size spec (`webclient/src/game/CardFace.tsx:182-210`) was designed for BattlefieldTile's `aspect-square w-full` slot, where `height: 100%` resolves against a definite parent box. Tabletop's render chain (motion.div → HoverCardDetail span → button → CardFace) had NO ancestor with an explicit height, so `height: 100%` resolved against a `0` parent and the card collapsed. Fix: pin the motion.div to `--card-size-medium` (80px) wide × derived height (112px tall via 5/7 aspect). Honors podShrink's token override path. T1 (bucket footprint unchanged), T3 (CardFace still rendering full Scryfall art) all unaffected. variant=current unchanged. All 1156+ tests green (TabletopBuckets suite still passes 8/8). Also surfaced the orchestrator-deploy gap that the user encountered on the way to this report — Vercel project env had `VITE_FEATURE_REDESIGN=""` (empty), so the bundle baked REDESIGN=false and PlayerFrameRedesigned / floating dock / slim phase rail / collapsed sidepanel never activated; all redesigned chrome was fenced behind the global REDESIGN flag. Workaround: shell-env override `VITE_FEATURE_REDESIGN=true` for builds. Permanent fix is to set the value correctly in Vercel project settings; documented for the user. |
| fix/variant-tabletop-bucket-overflow + local-pod-shrink (F1, 2026-05-04) | Trivial (TabletopBuckets.tsx — `overflow-x-auto` on cards-row + scaled peek margin via `[&>*+*]:[margin-left:calc(-1*var(--card-size-medium,80px)*0.6)]`; Battlefield.tsx — apply `computePodCardSizeVars(meBfCount)` to the bottom-pod wrapper for variant=tabletop) | Builder | All critics skipped — direct response to audit findings H4 and H5 from the live-game bug audit. H4: bucket cards beyond visible width were silently clipped because `overflow-hidden` on the bucket plus `flex-shrink: 0` on each card. With Commander board sizes (8-15 lands) cards 4+ became unreachable for click/target/combat. Fix: add `overflow-x-auto` so the spec's `shrink → stack → scroll` adaptation chain has its scroll fallback; also scale the peek margin with the active `--card-size-medium` token so podShrink's scale-down keeps proportional 40% visible-strip per card. H5: opponent pods applied `computePodCardSizeVars` but the local bottom pod did not, so the user's own crowded board overflowed silently while opponents' equivalent boards shrank to fit. Fix: apply the same shrink computation to bottom-pod wrapper. T1 ✓ (bucket footprint unchanged; cards adapt via scroll + shrink). variant=current unchanged. All 1157 tests green. |

## Process amendment 2026-04-30 — picture-catalog as load-bearing critic reference

After slice 70-K shipped with the row-stretch carry-over bug
(picture-catalog §2.1 specs "rows fixed, cards shrink"; the
implementation kept slice-53's `flex-wrap`), the user flagged
that critics were not consistently referencing the canonical
visual spec.

**Standing requirement going forward:** every critic dispatched
on a redesign-push slice (70-I → 70-Z + any future visual work)
loads `docs/design/picture-catalog.md` and reviews per the
template at `docs/decisions/critic-checklist-redesign.md`.
Critic reports that don't cite catalog clauses get re-dispatched.

This applies retroactively to slice 70-K.1 (the row-stretch fix
slice that landed concurrent with this amendment) and forward.

The slice plan's "Definition of done" (slice-plan-redesign-2026-04-29.md)
was updated to make this checkpoint the second item — directly
after "implementation matches catalog" — to surface it during
sign-off review.

| 70-Z.4 | Standard (impact tier C: dust crumple / bright dissolve / board-wipe ripple + picture-catalog Region 7 amendment; per critic-checklist-redesign.md) | Technical+Graphical (combined parallel post-builder), UI/UX | UX-only (`covered by UI/UX` — interaction model unchanged from 70-Z.3) | **3 CRITICAL fixes pre-commit (post-builder critic redesign):** (Tech-CRIT-1) the original implementation read `exitKindByCardId` inside `BattlefieldRowGroup`'s `permanents.map`, but AnimatePresence snapshots a child's exit props at the LAST render where it was PRESENT — by the time `creature_died` fires, the dying card has already been removed from `permanents`, so its motion.div's exit props were captured BEFORE the impact event fired (no impact branch). The keyframe never played. **Redesigned**: moved impact visuals INTO `CardAnimationLayer` as a fixed-position `ImpactOverlay` anchored to the dying tile's captured bbox (resolved at event-handler time via `[data-card-id]` while the tile is still in the DOM, since Zustand's synchronous subscribe path runs BEFORE React's re-render prunes the tile). The original tile's default B-glide exit runs in parallel beneath the overlay; the overlay paints the disintegration above it. Auto-cleanup via setTimeout matched to keyframe duration. Closes both Tech-CRIT-1 (visual never rendered) and Tech-CRIT-2 (exitKindByCardId map leaked forever, causing flicker re-summons to render as dust). (UI/UX-CRIT-1) `BoardWipeRipple` base size was 200px scaled 1.1× = 220px localized bloom, NOT the catalog's promised "screen pulse synced with the wave of disintegrations." Bumped base to 2000px so the keyframe's 0→1.1 scale produces a viewport-wide gold shockwave. (UI/UX-CRIT-4) `BoardWipeRipple` resolved its epicenter via `[data-player-id]` which has multiple matches on the local pod's slot-split (rows + frame both emit it). Switched to `[data-portrait-target-player-id]` which is unique per player. Same selector used by combat arrows (slice 70-N) for consistency. **2 IMPORTANT fixes pre-commit:** (Tech-IMP-1 / UI/UX-CRIT-2) `BOARD_WIPE_STAGGER_MS` was defined and `ImpactParticles` accepted `staggerMs`, but `BattlefieldRowGroup` never plumbed the index — every dust field fired at t=0. The redesigned `board_wipe` handler now indexes `evt.cardIds` and re-writes each `ActiveImpact` with `staggerMs = idx * BOARD_WIPE_STAGGER_MS`; per-permanent dust fields stagger outward from the epicenter so the wave reads. (UI/UX-IMP-2) the dust-crumple keyframe was opacity-fade + blur-down + scale-down only — read as "card fading out" (semantically closer to bouncing-to-hand) rather than "killed." Added a 0–8% impact-flash frame (brightness 1→1.4, scale 1→1.04) so combat damage reads as the killing blow before the dissolution begins. **2 NICE-TO-HAVE picked up:** updated catalog §7.3 to document the layer-overlay architecture + ripple base size + impact-flash; added a "Why a layer overlay (not in-tile keyframe)" subsection so future maintainers don't try to re-attach the keyframe to the dying motion.div. **Catalog Region 7 amendment landed in this slice** (~120 lines covering all three tiers + reduced-motion + z-ladder + counter-detection limitation). **Deferred:** (UI/UX-IMP-1) particle overflow past slot bounds — particles drift outside aspect-square containers; defer until live-test reveals whether they're visually noisy on neighboring tiles. (UI/UX-IMP-3) flicker-effect (Cloudshift / Restoration Angel) interplay with the impact overlay — overlay's bbox is captured pre-flicker, but Framer's layoutId glide for the re-summoned tile runs in parallel; minor visual hitch only on flicker, defer. (UI/UX-NTH-3) `setTimeout(onComplete)` in BoardWipeRipple is throttled in background tabs — defer to slice 70-Z.5 polish if it surfaces. |

| fix/auth-residual-oracle-closure (F22, 2026-05-04) | Trivial (single-conditional addition in `AuthService.login`) | Builder | All critics skipped — user-reported regression-style fix; the bug was visible in production after F21 flag-flip. Pre-F22 flow: when an unregistered username received a non-empty password, the flow fell through to upstream's anon path which ignores the password (authenticationActivated=false), creating an anon-by-name session. Two harms: enumeration oracle (registered → 401, unregistered → 200) AND UX trap (user thinks they're authenticated, actually anon-by-name). Fix: when `stored == null` AND password is non-empty AND username is non-empty, reject with 401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS — same wire shape as the wrong-password path so an attacker can't tell registered from unregistered. Empty-password + named user preserves the documented guest-by-name flow; empty username preserves server-generated guest. **Two new regression tests** pin both behaviors (`login_unregisteredUsernameWithPassword_returns401` + `login_unregisteredUsernameWithoutPassword_returns200Anon`). WebApi 426 run / 0 failures. JVM bounced; verified live: bogus26637 + hunter2hunter2 → 401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS; bogus26637 + (empty) → 200 isAnonymous=true. T-rules N/A. Closes the F21 audit's Sec B1 residual gap completely; the multi-class oracle is now fully unified on the wire. |
| feat/auth-readiness-milestone (F21, 2026-05-04) | Architectural (multi-component cross-stack: server-side validation tightening + new in-memory tracker class + Javalin before-filter for security headers + Java NFKC normalization on register+verify + reflection self-test at construction + wire-shape unification + orchestrator flag-flip + integration tests covering each fix) | Builder + multi-agent audit (Security / Correctness / UX) ran prior; F21 ships their findings. Critics integrated end-to-end via the audit-driven priority list in `docs/decisions/auth-roadmap.md`. **Items shipped:** F21.1 — username length cap aligned with upstream `maxUserNameLength=14` (resolves the F19 "flip-blocker" which was a length-validation mismatch, not an architectural bug). F21.2 — generic register-failure response with no enumeration oracle (collapsed USERNAME_TAKEN + would-be EMAIL_TAKEN into single 409 REGISTRATION_FAILED, dropped username echo). F21.3 — per-username login-attempt lockout via new `LoginAttemptTracker` (5 fails → 15 min, exponential backoff up to 24h, in-memory ConcurrentHashMap keyed by lowercase username). F21.4 — security response headers via Javalin before-filter (HSTS / X-Content-Type-Options / X-Frame-Options / Referrer-Policy / Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy). F21.5 — password polish (128-char max cap to prevent DoS amplification on the SHA-256 × 1024 loop, NFKC normalization on both register and verify so composed/decomposed Unicode forms interoperate). F21.6 — startup self-test `verifyReflectionShapeOrFail()` in AuthService constructor; throws IllegalStateException at boot on upstream AuthorizedUser shape drift. F21.7 — collapsed wire shape (PASSWORD_REQUIRED → INVALID_CREDENTIALS, identical message to wrong-password) closing the residual login-error oracle (server log retains telemetry distinction via LOG.info reason= field). F21 deploy — `playtest-up.sh` Phase 3 now exports `XMAGE_REGISTRATION_ENABLED=true`. **Items deferred** (defense-in-depth, not exploitation-blockers): Argon2id migration (upstream-touching, Sec A1), token off localStorage (CSRF rework, Sec C2), H2 at-rest encryption (upstream-touching, Sec A6), HIBP k-anonymity check (network call in auth path, Sec A5), email verification (needs SMTP, Sec D1). All recorded in `docs/decisions/auth-roadmap.md` F21 section as queued follow-up slices. **Live verification post-bounce:** `registrationEnabled: true`, full register→login round-trip → 200 isAnonymous=false, all 5 security headers on every response, lockout fires after 5 wrong-password attempts. | feat/ai-tier1-polish-closeout (AI-8.6, 2026-05-05) | Standard (5 polish fixes layered on already-validated subclass surface; H1 comment-only; H2-H5 small additive logic per file; ~30 LOC net change across all 4 sibling files) | Builder | All critics skipped — Standard tier closing the H-items deferred from the AI-8.5 parallel critic pass; each fix maps 1:1 to a finding the critics already produced. Technical (`covered by AI-8.5 review` — same surface, fixes are the critics' own findings). Magic-rules (`covered by AI-8.5 review` — H1 verifies CR 903.8 watcher semantics; H3 tightens self-wipe rule still grounded in CR 903.4 board-state economics; H4 narrows to outcomes never mis-tagged on opponent-benefit cards). UI / UX / Graphical (`no UI surface`). | **5 H-items closed:** **H1 (Magic-rules):** verified upstream `CommanderPlaysCountWatcher.watch` increments on `SPELL_CAST` in `Zone.COMMAND` AFTER the prior cast went on stack — `getPlaysCount` reflects RESOLVED prior casts, never the upcoming one. Tax = 2 × prior-casts (CR 903.8) so the threshold-8 filter correctly refuses the 5th cast attempt (4 prior deaths and recasts → tax 8 on the 5th). No math change; explicit comment added to `CommanderActionFilter.isUnsafeCommanderRecast` so the next reader doesn't re-derive it. **H2 (Technical):** `selectAttackers` partial-declaration leak. Old `declareAll` returned false on mid-loop failure but left attackers[0..k-1] declared in `Combat`. Caller deferred to `super.selectAttackers` which then re-enumerated on top of dirty partial state — would either double-declare or silently mis-attack. New `declareAll` tracks declared-attacker UUIDs and calls `combat.removeAttacker(id, game)` (verified at `Combat.java:1804`) for each on rollback so super sees a clean slate. **H3 (Magic-rules + Technical):** asymmetric wipe over-refusal. Old rule `ourCreatures >= max(opp creature counts)` refused wipes when the loss was actually positive-EV for us (us=4 vs. opps=[4,2,0]: lose 4 of ours, table loses 6 — clearly should wipe; old rule blocked it because max=4 and 4>=4). New rule: `ourCreatures > sum(opponentCreatures)` — refuse only when our loss is strictly bigger than the rest of the table's combined loss. Ties go to "wipe" (clearing the table is tempo-positive for us at parity). **H4 (Magic-rules):** lowest-life heuristic over-fired on `Outcome.Detriment` spells where the targeted player BENEFITS. The Outcome enum tags Detriment as `good=false` (= bad for target), but the tag is overloaded — some cards apply it to "target opponent draws three cards" / similar opp-benefit effects. Tightened the heuristic gate from `!outcome.isGood()` to membership in an `EnumSet` of unambiguously-opponent-harming outcomes: `{Damage, LoseLife, Discard, Sacrifice}`. Spells with other Outcomes fall through to super (upstream's iterator-first pick) — strictly no worse than pre-AI-8.0 for the dropped cases. **H5 (Integration):** empty-tree-bug WARN message reported `game.getState().getTurnNum()` = the turn we just transitioned INTO, not the turn the bug fired ON. Added a separate `lastObservedTurnNum` field captured BEFORE the boundary transition; passed into `maybeWarnEmptyTree` so the message names the right turn. First-ever observation guarded by `priorTurnNum < 0` early-return. **L1-L3 nits remain deferred** — no specs were captured at AI-8.5 time; treating as cleanup-as-encountered. **Why "Standard" not "Trivial":** H2 is a correctness fix in shipped code (combat state mutation across error path), and H3/H4 change behavior of already-shipped heuristics. Trivial tier is reserved for "applying critic's own findings" with no behavior risk; these have small but non-zero behavior risk. **T-rules:** N/A — server-side AI logic, no tabletop variant surface touched. **Schema impact:** none. **Verification:** WebApi 446/446 BUILD SUCCESS (no test count change — pure refinements; existing reflection pins continue to cover the override surface, internal whitebox not pinned for rebase-fragility reasons). Live JVM picks up via `playtest-up.sh` bounce. **Tier 1 fully closed — Tier 2 (multi-opponent evaluator, hand quality, removal conservation, smarter mulligans) queued in `docs/design/ai-upgrades.md`.** |
| refactor/ai-extract-and-critic-fixes (AI-8.5, 2026-05-05) | Architectural (post-Tier-1 critic-driven slice — 3-helper extract resolves CLAUDE.md hard-cap violation + 5 critical bug fixes from the parallel agentic review) | Builder + parallel 3-critic pass (technical / Magic-rules / xmage-AI integration, dispatched in parallel after AI-8.4 shipped) | All critics already ran — this slice IS the critic-driven fixer pass. Findings drove the slice scope; no fresh critic dispatch needed for the fixes themselves since each fix maps 1:1 to a critic finding. UI / UX / Graphical (`no UI surface`). | **5 critical fixes + structural extract resolving 6 issues from the AI-8.0 → AI-8.4 review.** **C1 (Magic-rules expert):** `commanderDamageDealtTo` summed across our commanders — CR 903.14a is per-commander. With partners A=10/B=12 the helper returned 22 → AI thought opp was dead → wasted removal on healthy seat. Renamed to `CommanderTargetingHeuristic.maxCommanderDamageDealtBy` returning `max` not `sum`. **C2 (Magic-rules):** `tryLethalShortCircuit` only ran life-loss math; AI-8.1 tracks commander damage but AI-8.4 ignored it. Added `findCommanderDamageLethal` path: if our unblockable commander has enough power to push tally to 21, declare it as lethal. Conservative — requires unblockable since blocked commander damage to player is 0. **C3 (Magic-rules):** lethal short-circuit committed whole board against Platinum Angel/Phyrexian Unlife. Added `opp.canLose(game)` pre-check before declaring. **C4 (Integration expert):** human-takeover hazard — when a human took control of an AI seat via the proxy, our overrides still fired and could declare attackers without asking the human. Added `isGameUnderControl()` gate to all three overrides (`chooseTarget`, `act`, `selectAttackers`). **C5 (Technical + Integration):** `chooseTarget` called `target.add()` without `target.canTarget()` validation — hexproof from an aura we missed could cause silent no-op while we returned true → spell fizzles. Added `target.canTarget(playerId, picked, source, game)` pre-check + `target.getTargets().contains(picked)` post-add check (target.add returns void; can't trust it succeeded). **C6 (Technical):** file at 631 LOC, 26% over 500 hard cap. Extracted into 4 sibling files: `CommanderComputerPlayer7.java` (218 LOC, orchestrator + telemetry + overrides), `CommanderTargetingHeuristic.java` (146 LOC, chooseTarget bias), `CommanderActionFilter.java` (194 LOC, act() filter), `CommanderLethalShortCircuit.java` (192 LOC, selectAttackers helper). Threshold deliberately conservative — lower thresholds (6 = died 3+ times) would over-restrict legitimate finisher recasts. (2) `isCounterproductiveBoardWipe` — drops `SpellAbility` whose any effect class simple-name contains `DestroyAll` / `DamageAll` / `ExileAll`, gated on (a) we have ≥ 2 creatures, (b) our creature count ≥ each opponent's creature count. Threshold tuned high enough that normal turn cycles (untap/upkeep/draw/combat passes) don't trigger. **Why this slice was the right next step (vs. items #3/#4/#5):** items 3 and 4 (recast-tax + don't-wipe-own-board) and item 5 (lethal short-circuit) each need deeper hook investigation that wouldn't fit in a focused slice — recast-tax has no obvious cast-decision interception point, board-wipes are detected only by inspecting effect classes deep in the spell, lethal short-circuit needs attacker enumeration via `CombatUtil.canKillOpponent`. Telemetry is the simplest tractable Tier 1 item AND surfaces the data we need to know whether further investment in items 3-5 is justified — if the empty-tree bug fires constantly, item-5-style mitigation is high-priority; if it rarely fires, our skill=4 mitigation suffices and we can focus on Tier 2 instead. **Architecture:** overrides `protected void act(Game)` (declared in `ComputerPlayer6.java:172`). Tracks turn boundaries via `(turnNum, activePlayerId)` hash so multiple AI seats in the same game don't conflate counters. Counter state lives on the player instance (per-game by construction since players are constructed per-game). **Defensive:** the `actions` queue (protected field on `ComputerPlayer6`) is null-checked. Copy constructor preserves counter state across simulation cloning (probably moot since simulation paths short-circuit, but harmless). **Test pin:** `actOverride_isPresent` test asserts the override exists with the right signature via reflection — if upstream renames or re-signatures `act()`, this fires before the telemetry silently dies. **Verification:** WebApi 444/444 BUILD SUCCESS (was 443/443 at AI-8.1; 1 new test). | feat/ai-commander-damage-finisher-bias (AI-8.1, 2026-05-05) | Standard (extends the AI-8.0 chooseTarget override; new `commanderDamageDealtTo` helper reading upstream `CommanderInfoWatcher`; rename `pickLowestLifeOpponentIfApplicable` → `pickThreatTargetIfApplicable` to reflect the unified score) | Builder | All critics skipped — Standard tier extends an already-shipped override surface that the AI-8.0 critic pass + parallel pre-flight research already validated. Pile on the closer kill. **Why this is the right next slice:** both research agents independently picked it as a Tier 1 priority right after lowest-life-targeting. ---

## Skip-rationale categories

Use one of these short tags in the rationale column to keep the log scannable:

- **`no UI surface`** — slice is server-side or pure logic; UI critic has nothing to assess
- **`no motion`** — slice ships no new animations / transitions; Graphical has nothing to assess
- **`covered by X`** — another specialist's coverage absorbs this angle (e.g. GUI usually covered by UX + UI on game-table slices)
- **`foundation slice`** — slice ships infrastructure consumers will use later; UX angle deferred to consumer slice
- **`spec-locked`** — visual / interaction decisions are pre-decided in a design doc; specialist would have nothing new to flag
- **`over-applied`** — full panel ran when tier table says fewer would have sufficed (record but don't repeat)

---

## How to use this log

**Before starting a slice:** check the tier table in CLAUDE.md and pick the matrix. Note any deferrals here BEFORE the build starts so the rationale is captured at decision time, not retrofitted.

**At commit time:** add the row to "Active log" above. The "Critical findings caught" column is the value-added evidence — empty rows are a signal that the slice may not have needed those critics at all.

**During audits:** scan the "Specialists skipped" column for clusters. If the same specialist gets skipped 5+ slices in a row, the tier table may be wrong (or the slices are all genuinely outside that specialist's domain).

All blockers + relevant notables fixed in single fixer pass. New class `CommanderSimulatePriorityOverride` extracted to keep CommanderSearchTreeOverride under 400-LOC soft cap; chain is now ControllableProxy → CommanderSimulatePriorityOverride → CommanderSearchTreeOverride → CommanderComputerPlayer7. Memory captured: `feedback_canLose_isInGame_checklist.md` codifies the canLose+isInGame gate for any future life-state-reading AI code. AI-8.5 lesson now formally documented across two slices' worth of repeat-bugs. |

| FB#8′ (2026-05-05, chat-promotion path — necessary but not sufficient) | Standard (architectural data-flow correction) | Builder only | Technical (`covered by tests` — 3 new regression tests in store.test.ts assert MessageType=GAME chat promotes to gameLog with shouldKeepGameLogEntry filter, MessageType=TALK does not, MessageType=GAME phase noise still drops; full store suite 93/93 green), UI (`no UI surface` — pure data routing in the store reducer; existing GameLog / GameLogWindow components unchanged), Magic-rules (`no rules logic` — engine-side flow tracing) | Engine `informPlayers(text)` → `EventType.INFO` (GameImpl.java:3182) → `chatManager.broadcast(MessageType.GAME, ...)` (GameController.java:125-128). The wire `gameInform` callback (GAME_UPDATE_AND_INFORM) is only fired by `GameSessionWatcher.inform()` from `informOthers()` for "Waiting for player X" status text. So all card-play / damage / life-change content lives in chat with messageType=GAME, not in gameInform. Fix: `reduceChatMessage` now also promotes MessageType=GAME chat into `gameLog` (gated by the same `shouldKeepGameLogEntry` filter so phase-noise STATUS messages still drop). **NB:** post-deploy retest revealed this STILL didn't fix the empty log. The chat frames weren't reaching the client at all because `GameStreamHandler` never called `chatManager.joinChat` — see FB#8″ for the actual data-arrival fix. This row's webclient code remains correct (and necessary): it's the right destination, but the data couldn't reach it. |

| FB#8″ (2026-05-05, **ACTUAL** root cause — `GameStreamHandler` never joined the chat session) | Architectural (3 prior fixes targeted downstream of the actual bug; missed integration touchpoint between WebApi and engine chat session) | Builder only | Technical (`covered by tests + live retest` — Java compile clean; the one-call delta is small enough that adding a unit test with mocked ChatManager is the next polish; user retest after JVM restart is the contract test for now), UI (`no UI surface` — server-side WS subscription wiring), Magic-rules (`no rules logic`) | **Root cause: GameStreamHandler.bindGameChatId set the WS-context delivery filter attribute (line 218) but never called `chatManager.joinChat(chatId, userId)` to register the user in the upstream `ChatSession.users` map.** Without that registration, `ChatSession.broadcast()` (Mage.Server/.../ChatSession.java:183) iterated past the user every time → callback never fired → no `chatMessage` wire frame for the WS → empty `chatMessages` bucket → empty gameLog. The lobby (RoomStreamHandler.java:133) DOES call joinChat, which is why lobby chat works. The legacy Swing client also calls joinChat upstream-side. Webclient missed it during the chat slice and shipped silently broken. Fix adds `chatManager.joinChat(chatId, userId)` and a `resolveUserId` helper inside `bindGameChatId`. **Major debugging lesson:** 4 fixes attempted (filter relax FB#8, chat-promotion FB#8′, env-var redeploy fix, console diagnostic) all failed because none of them verified data was reaching the client at all. The user explicitly asked "can you read into the server or build a tool" — that prompt forced the recon path that found `joinChat`. **Pattern for next time:** for "feature X is empty / silent" bugs, the recon order MUST be (1) verify data arrival via devtools/server-log/observer BEFORE (2) tuning data filters. Compare to nearest sibling that works (here: lobby chat) — the delta is the bug. New memory `feedback_data_arrival_before_filter.md` captures this. |

New DuplicateStackContainer renders N stacked duplicates as N independently-clickable cards with 16px peek offsets — closes the long-standing "can't pick back-of-stack token in declare-blockers / can't choose which Forest taps for mana" bug class. | FB#3 (2026-05-05, Mirari's Wake / AddManaOfAnyTypeProducedEffect — wider choice on multi-color lands) | Architectural (touches upstream shared effect class with 16-card blast radius + updates 2 existing upstream tests + adds 1 new upstream test; engine-only, no schema/wire change but upstream-rebase-impacting) | Builder only — user explicitly directed "do A and move on" after I surfaced the rules-interpretation ambiguity + the test-update scope; user is the Magic-rules authority for this project | Magic-rules (`user-authority decision` — I stopped mid-implementation when I realized I couldn't definitively resolve the strict-vs-loose-reading question of "any type that land produced" without authoritative ruling access; user reviewed both interpretations + the 16-card scope + the 2-test-update cost and chose the loose-reading fix), Technical (`covered by tests + clear mechanical pattern` — union-over-mana-abilities is a single straightforward loop; existing engine helpers `getActivatedManaAbilities(Zone.BATTLEFIELD)` + `getNetMana(game)` provide the data; defensive fallback to `types.copy()` if no mana abilities found; 3 new MirarisWakeTest cases pin the 3 paths (Tundra→prompt, Tundra→pick same color, Forest→auto-pick); 2 updated AddManaOfAnyTypeProducedTest cases (`testGemstoneCavern` adds Vorinclex-trigger setChoice; `testCastWithGemstoneCavern` clarified — Vorinclex is in hand at Gemstone tap so no trigger fires there)), AI (`out of scope — this is rules code, not AI`) | **Initial recon stop-and-confirm:** my first pass implemented the loose-reading fix, then I hit 2 existing upstream tests that encode the strict reading + 16-card blast radius + rules ambiguity. Stopped and presented user 3 paths (narrow Mirari-only override / wide shared-effect fix / defer for ruling). User chose path A: fix the shared effect properly. **Implementation:** `produceMana` now computes the choice set from the union of every activated mana ability's `getNetMana(game)` on the tapped permanent, instead of the single tap output (`types`). Tundra tapped for {W} now offers {W} + {U}; Forest tapped for {G} still auto-picks {G}; Gemstone Caverns with luck counter offers all 5 colors. Defensive fallback to `types.copy()` if the permanent has lost all mana abilities by trigger resolution time. **Two upstream test updates:** `testGemstoneCavern` (Vorinclex on battlefield, Gemstone tapped) needed an additional `setChoice(playerB, "White")` for Vorinclex's trigger now that Gemstone's any-color ability returns 5 colors via getNetMana. `testCastWithGemstoneCavern` did NOT need an update — Vorinclex is in hand when Gemstone is tapped, so no trigger fires there (caught the misclassification in the first test run + corrected with a clarifying comment). **One new test file** `MirarisWakeTest.java` (3 cases pinning the FB#3 contract). **Build flow note:** `mvn -pl Mage.Tests` doesn't rebuild upstream `Mage` — first test run failed because old `AddManaOfAnyTypeProducedEffect.class` was still in the test classpath. Required `mvn -pl Mage,Mage.Sets,Mage.Tests install -DskipTests` first, then `mvn -pl Mage.Tests test -Dtest=...`. **Upstream-rebase impact:** documented in a comment block above the changed `produceMana` body so future rebase preserves the union-over-abilities approach. **Final result:** 9/9 affected tests green pre-commit. User smoke-tests on live to verify the actual gameplay matches expectation; if any of the other 14 sibling cards (Mana Flare, Heartbeat, Vorinclex, etc.) misbehave under the new wider-choice logic, revert via git. |

| INFRA-1 (2026-05-06, self-hosted runtime bundle: 3 Windows Services + Cloudflare Tunnel + watchdog) | Standard (single new surface — `mage-stack/` folder; ~942 LOC PowerShell across 9 files; no Java source touched, no wire-format change, no schema bump) | Builder only — user-driven slice with multiple stop-and-confirm checkpoints during scoping (token-vs-credentials pivot + dashboard navigation issues), user authorized each phase explicitly | Technical (`pre-build self-vetted via inline recon — see findings column`), UI / UX / Graphical (`no UI surface — pure ops`), Magic-rules (`no rules logic`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Recon-time discoveries that shrunk the slice:** (1) `/api/health` already exists at `WebApiServer.java:283` returning `{schemaVersion, status:"ready"}` — Java-side scope vanished entirely; the bundle has no Java source change. (2) Original CLAUDE.md "Defaults: classic server on 17171, WebApi REST on 18080, WebApi WS on 18081" was stale — Javalin serves REST + WebSocket on the same port (18080); only one Cloudflare hostname needed. (3) `.secrets/` was untracked-but-not-gitignored — token file at `F:\xmage\.secrets\cloudflare-tunnel-token.txt` was one accidental `git add .` away from being committed; fixed PRE-write of any script that reads it. **Mid-implementation pivots:** (P1) Cloudflare dashboard public-hostname flow has been reorganized away (Hostname routes Beta is for Zero Trust private, "Create new route" form needs CIDR not service URL, even Zero Trust Networks→Tunnels no longer shows the Public Hostname tab) — pivoted from token-based connector with dashboard ingress to credentials-file mode with local `cloudflared-config.yml`; cleaner long-term anyway. (P2) `mvn exec:java` (used by `run.sh`) is a bad fit for NSSM (slow cold-start, 3-process chain hard to PID-track); fat-JAR plugin in WebApi pom would break scope-lock; resolved with `mvn dependency:build-classpath` at install time + `java -cp @file` direct launch — keeps scope locked AND gets clean NSSM ops. **File-size discipline catch:** `install-service.ps1` initial draft hit 426 LOC (over 400 soft cap); refactor extracted `_helpers.ps1` (Write-Section/Step/Ok/Warn, Get-BundleRootFromScript, Read-BundleConfig, Format-Uptime, Install-NssmService) reused across all 7 other scripts AND DRY'd 3 near-identical NSSM service registrations into one parameterized helper — install-service.ps1 dropped to 351 LOC, all other scripts ≤88 LOC. **Critic-skip rationale:** `mage-stack/` is pure ops scripting with no race-condition surface beyond shell-process supervision (NSSM handles); no wire-format change (Java source untouched); no UI; no Magic-rules; no AI. Live install on the dev box is the contract test for the actual ops behavior. **Files:** 9 PowerShell + 3 config templates + 1 .gitignore + README + MIGRATION + ADR 0012 + this row + 2 deprecation header touches on `scripts/playtest-{up,down}.sh`. **Test plan executes post-commit on user's box:** install-service dry-run → 3 services green → force-kill JVM → NSSM auto-restart within 10s → suspend JVM via `Suspend-Process` → watchdog restart after ~90s → mage-redeploy round-trip → 1v1 fixture game completes after forced mid-game restart. |

| FB#13 (2026-05-07, token cards have no artwork — mapper substitutes imageNumber for the upstream "0" sentinel) | Standard (server-side mapper wire-content fix + 3 new Java unit tests; no schema bump, no upstream-tracked file modified) | Builder, Technical-via-recon (the diagnosis traced the full data path: TokenImpl.setExpansionSetCode + setImageNumber upstream → CardView.java:1037,1102 hardcodes cardNumber="0" + stores meaningful identifier in int imageNumber → our CardViewMapper.java:111 passed cardNumber verbatim and never read imageNumber → client scryfallImageUrl built /cards/<set>/0 which Scryfall 404s → blank token rectangles) | UI/UX (`no visual change beyond actual artwork now appearing — server-side fix, client renders unchanged`), Magic-rules (`the contract IS Scryfall's URL space; covered by the unit test asserting numeric translation`), Graphical (`no motion`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Root cause:** xmage's CardView constructor for tokens hardcodes the wire's collector-number string to "0" and stuffs the real Scryfall identifier into a separate `int imageNumber` field. Our CardViewMapper passed cardNumber through verbatim and never read imageNumber for cards (only for commanders, per slice 70-X.2). Client scryfallImageUrl built /cards/<set>/0 → 404 → blank rectangles. **Fix:** new private helper `resolveWireCardNumber(cv)` substitutes `String.valueOf(cv.getImageNumber())` when `cv.isToken()` is true; non-tokens pass through unchanged. One callsite swap (CardViewMapper.java:111). **Why no schema bump:** wire SHAPE unchanged (`cardNumber: string`); only value-content for the previously-broken token case. Same precedent as FB#11 multi-amount space-vs-comma. Zero deployed consumers depend on the "0" sentinel — every token URL built from it 404s today. **Tests:** new CardViewMapperTokenTest.java (3 cases: token w/ imageNumber=11 → wire "11"; non-token "161" pass-through; token w/ imageNumber=0 boundary documented as engine-side gap not mapper bug). Reflection helper walks parent class because cardNumber/expansionSetCode live on SimpleCardView while isToken/imageNumber live on CardView. **Verification:** 20/20 mapper tests green pre-commit; deploy via mage-redeploy (~60s WebApi bounce; tunnel + watchdog stay up; slice 70-H disconnect handling absorbs any active games). **Critic-skip rationale:** Magic-rules covered by direct test against Scryfall's URL contract; UI/UX is server-only with no client visual character change beyond the bug-fix itself; the technical analysis was the recon. **Tabletop rules:** N/A — universal server fix; affects current + tabletop variants equally; T5's "no engine/wire/schema" applies to *variant work*, not to a server-side data-content fix outside the variant-toggle scope. Followup deferred: backFace tokens (Phyrexian/Incubator from MOM) per TokenImpl.java:523/531 "manual setup needed" — flagged if surfaces. |

| FB#12 (2026-05-06, hand-minimize chevron occluded by CommanderDamageTracker — move to top-LEFT) | Trivial (1-className substitution + comment update) | Builder only — user-reported visual occlusion with the occluder identified by the user themselves | Technical (`covered by inline geometry recon — chevron at right-2/top-2 z-20 inside a z-30 fixed strip; tracker dock at fixed bottom-36 right-3 z-30 w-360px max-h-55vh; both z-30, tracker mounts later in DOM order so DOM-order tie-break wins`), UI/UX/Graphical (`single-pixel positional change, no visual character change`), Magic-rules / AI (`no rules / no AI`) | The chevron was added in commit 3fbf6619 (2026-05-04) and was always at top-right of MyHand. F2 (audit W6, 2026-05-04) added the floating CommanderDamageTracker dock for tabletop because side-panel-collapse hid the in-panel tracker; the dock landed at right-3 with the same z-30 as the hand strip's fixed container. DOM-mount order (tracker after hand region in GameTable) made the tracker win the tie-break and visually erase the chevron over a 360px-wide × 792px-tall corridor on the right. The right corridor is increasingly claimed (tracker + action dock + GameLog pill + local mana pool); moved chevron to top-LEFT where the only neighbours are the bottom-anchored local-command-zone and the inner-card-row "Empty hand" hint at left-3 top-3 (which is offset 140px right of the outer container's left edge). No collision. T1-T7 all preserved (overlay button, no zone/layout/wire change). Server stayed up — pure webclient bundle redeploy via Vercel; new bundle hash index-n5NrETvA.js verified live at modern-mage.com. Pre-existing typecheck errors unchanged (none in MyHand). 1223/1223 webclient tests green. Critic-skip rationale: positional className change with verified geometry; the contract test was the user's own confirmation in the next session if needed. |

| INFRA-3 (2026-05-06, mage-cors-refresh.ps1 + template bump for apex<->www CORS) | Trivial (single new ops script ~80 LOC + 1 line + 1 hint comment in config.json.template + README ops-manual update from 5 to 6 commands + memory note) | Builder only — ops scripting that mirrors the existing mage-redeploy.ps1 shape | Technical (`covered by inline recon — script is a 6-line nssm set + restart + health-poll, identical pattern to the existing mage-redeploy and exercised live by Path A's pasted equivalent before the script was even written; the user's antivirus rejected the temp-file shortcut earlier in the session, which is what motivated this checked-in version`), UI/UX/Graphical (`no UI surface — pure ops`), Magic-rules (`no rules logic`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Why this slice exists:** post-INFRA-2, Vercel was 307-redirecting `modern-mage.com -> www.modern-mage.com` by default (Vercel auto-adds the redirect when both apex + www are project custom domains). The CORS allow-list had the apex but not the www variant. Browser preflight from the redirected origin returned 200 with NO `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header → fetch threw → React app reported transport-level "Can't reach the server". Server-side curl succeeded all along, making this confusing to diagnose. **First fix attempt** was an admin temp-file PowerShell one-shot; user's AV correctly flagged the `[System.IO.Path]::GetTempFileName() + '.ps1'` + `Start-Process -Verb RunAs` combo as a malware-loader signature. Saved as feedback memory `feedback_no_temp_ps1_elevation.md` — never use that pattern again. **Actual fix delivered today:** (a) live runtime: edited `config.json.corsOrigins` to add `https://www.modern-mage.com`, then user pasted `nssm set MageWebApi AppEnvironmentExtra ... ; nssm restart MageWebApi` from their own admin PS terminal. Verified preflight returns proper ACAO header for www origin. (b) Durable: this slice — new `mage-cors-refresh.ps1` so the env-only refresh is one command without temp-file pattern; `config.json.template` updated with both apex + www in default corsOrigins + a `_corsOriginsHint` comment field warning of the redirect trap; `reference_cloudflare_tunnel_gotchas.md` extended with the new "Vercel apex<->www redirect breaks CORS" section + symptom signature + fix path. **Critic-skip rationale:** identical shape to mage-redeploy.ps1 + mage-status.ps1 (NSSM env manipulation + service restart + health poll), already-vetted helpers from `_helpers.ps1`, no new external dependencies. The contract test was the user pasting Path A's equivalent commands and seeing health/CORS go green — covered. **Files:** new `mage-stack/scripts/mage-cors-refresh.ps1` (~80 LOC), edits to `mage-stack/config/config.json.template`, `mage-stack/README.md` (5- -> 6-command manual), this row, two memory updates. **Note:** `config.json` itself is gitignored (per-machine), so the runtime fix from Path A is not committed and persists on the user's box only. |

| FB#11 (2026-05-06, MultiAmountDialog wire-format fix — space-join not comma-join) | Standard (one-line value-format fix in webclient + 2 docstring updates + 2 unit-test assertion updates + 1 new Java contract test; no schema bump, no upstream-tracked file modified) | Builder, Technical-via-recon (the diagnosis WAS the technical critic — traced the wire path webclient → handlePlayerResponse → sendPlayerString → HumanPlayer.response → MultiAmountType.parseAnswer and confirmed the separator mismatch via the legacy Swing client's join in PickMultiNumberDialog.java:160) | UI/UX (`no visual change — internal value-format only`), Graphical (`no motion`), Magic-rules (`the contract IS upstream's parser; round-trip test against parseAnswer is the rules check`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Root cause** was a docstring-vs-engine-parser divergence: WebMultiAmountInfo Javadoc + MultiAmountDialog Javadoc both promised comma-joined response, but `MultiAmountType.parseAnswer` (Mage/src/main/java/mage/constants/MultiAmountType.java:171) splits on `" "`. Comma-joined `"3,2,1".split(" ")` → `["3,2,1"]`, parseInt fails → `[0]`, isGoodValues rejects on `values.size() != constraints.size()`, HumanPlayer logs "GUI return wrong MultiAmountType values" and loops. Trample / counter-removal / split-mana modals were all silently broken end-to-end since slice 70-X.14 Wave 3 shipped. **Why no test caught it:** webclient unit tests asserted `'2,1,0'` against the dialog's own (wrong) docstring — round-tripping through the real engine parser was never exercised. Upstream's `Mage.Tests` `TestPlayer.getMultiAmountWithIndividualConstraints` overrides the Java method and bypasses `parseAnswer` entirely. Mock-vs-real divergence textbook case. **Fix:** `values.join(',')` → `values.join(' ')` in MultiAmountDialog.tsx + parallel docstring + test-assertion updates. **Contract test pin:** new `MultiAmountWireContractTest.java` in WebApi (3 cases: space-joined accepts, multi-digit values round-trip, comma-joined regression-pin). Lives in new `mage.webapi.contract` package alongside existing `CombatFlowContractTest`. **No `schemaVersion` bump** — the JSON shape (`playerResponse{kind:'string', value:string}`) is unchanged; this is value-content alignment with an existing engine contract. No deployed comma-format consumer to break (modal never actually worked). **Verification:** `mvn -f Mage.Server.WebApi/pom.xml test -Dtest=MultiAmountWireContractTest` → 3/3 green; webclient `pnpm test` → 1223/1223 green. Pre-existing typecheck errors in `GameDialog.test.tsx` / `Lobby.test.tsx` / `LobbyChat.tsx` / `useModalA11y.test.tsx` are unrelated to this slice (already on the queued typecheck-cleanup list). **Lesson learned (memory-worthy):** docstring-pinned wire formats need a round-trip test that imports the consumer (here: upstream's parser). Pattern is now established by this contract-package precedent — apply to remaining Wave-3 dialogs (pile-pick, ability-picker) on next critical pass. |

| feat/token-scryfall-url-isToken-flag (2026-05-08, schema 1.32: tokens still don't render artwork post-FB#13) | Standard (cross-stack: 1 new wire field + schema bump 1.31→1.32 + webclient URL builder branch; companion to FB#13 covering MTG TOK subset that FB#13's imageNumber-forwarding couldn't reach) | Builder + Technical critic (post-builder, against the diff) | UI/UX (`covered by Technical + visual outcome is binary "art shows up or not"`), Magic-rules (`no rules logic — Scryfall URL convention`), AI (`no AI surface`), Graphical (`no motion`) | **Why this slice exists:** user reported Krenko's Goblin tokens still rendered without artwork after FB#13 shipped. Recon traced the data path end-to-end and found two compounding gaps FB#13 missed: (1) most `tokens-database.txt` rows have empty image-number columns, so engine stamps `imageNumber=0` for typical TOK tokens — FB#13 dutifully forwarded "0" to the wire; (2) Scryfall's URL space prefixes token-set codes with `t` (tdom, trna, tgrn) — distinct from regular set codes — and the webclient's `scryfallImageUrl` never added the prefix. Net effect: `cards/dom/0` returned 404 (or worse, the wrong DOM card #0 if it existed). Legacy Swing client solved this with a hardcoded `set/name → URL` map in `Mage.Client/.../ScryfallImageSupportTokens.java`; our pipeline never replicated it. **Fix:** added `boolean isToken` to `WebCardView` (schema 1.32 minor bump — additive, clients warn but continue per the docs/schema/CHANGELOG.md versioning policy); WebApi mapper emits `cv != null && cv.isToken()`; webclient `scryfallImageUrl` branches on `card.isToken` and routes through Scryfall's `/cards/named?exact=<name>&set=t<setcode>&format=image` endpoint — eliminates the imageNumber dependency entirely, no hardcoded URL map needed. **Critic findings caught (all fixed pre-commit):** (CRITICAL) the new required `isToken: boolean` field on the WebCardView TS interface broke 3 webclient construction sites — `sourceResolvers.ts:120`, `PlayerFrameRedesigned.tsx:323`, `store.ts:250` — added `isToken: false` literal to each (3 pre-existing source-rot sites at MyHand.tsx / battlefieldRows.test.ts / ZoneIcon.test.tsx left alone — those were broken by the 1.26 source-field rot before my slice and aren't in scope). (NOTABLE) initial implementation used `?fuzzy=` but rest of webclient consistently uses `?exact=` — switched for consistency since token names are well-formed. (NOTABLE) double-`t` prefix risk for sets whose code starts with T (THB, THS, etc.) — verified via Scryfall's `/sets/tthb` API that `tthb` IS the correct token-set code distinct from `thb`, and the legacy Swing client's hardcoded URLs confirm (`THB/Goat → cards/tthb/1`). Added a test case pinning the double-t handling. (NIT) added apostrophe-in-name test (e.g. "Saheeli's Lattice" → `exact=Saheeli's%20Lattice` since encodeURIComponent passes apostrophes through unencoded per RFC 3986). (NIT) hardcoded `1.31` literal in `AuthServiceRegisterTest.java:120` would have failed CI — caught by full mvn test run, fixed reactively. **Verification:** WebApi 497/497 + webclient 1242/1242 (12 new) tests green pre-commit. Pre-existing typecheck errors in MyHand / battlefieldRows / ZoneIcon (1.26 source rot) and PlayerFrameRedesigned (framer-motion exactOptionalPropertyTypes) unchanged — same pattern as the recent combat-arrow slice. **Out of scope** (deferred + flagged): XMAGE-namespaced tokens (Copy/Morph/Manifest) whose set code can't be `t`-prefixed usefully — those still 404 and need separate URL pre-resolution. Banana / un-databased tokens (no row in `tokens-database.txt`) likewise still 404 — engine-data gap. **Schema bump rationale:** rule 4 ("All four, or none") triggered: bumped SchemaVersion 1.31→1.32 + CHANGELOG entry + zod schema with `default(false)` for forward-compat + WebCardView TS interface — all four. **Forward-compat:** old client (1.31) ignores the new field; new client (1.32) gets `default(false)` from zod when an old server is mid-deploy → renders via the broken old path same as today. No regressions on stale-server connections. |

| feat/combat-arrow-fan-and-hover-isolate (2026-05-08, FB-queued: combat arrows clumped at shared defender) | Standard (single visual surface — SVG overlay; extracts CombatArrows from over-cap StackZone.tsx; no wire-format change, no schemaVersion bump) | Builder + Technical critic (post-builder, against the diff) | UI (`covered by Technical + visual surface is geometric/opacity-only — no new visual encoding, no token / palette change`), UX (`covered by Technical + scope is hover-isolation + endpoint-fan; the interaction model is well-known from MTG Arena`), Graphical (`opacity-only fade — exempt from prefers-reduced-motion per WCAG SC 2.3.3 which targets translation/parallax/autoplay; no motion-sickness surface`), Magic-rules / AI (`no rules logic / no AI surface`) | **What shipped:** Endpoint-fan splays N arrows that share a target along the perpendicular to each arrow's source→target line by `(i − (N−1)/2) × 24px`, so 3 attackers on 1 defender produce 3 distinct arrowheads instead of overlapping at the same pixel. Hover-isolation: document-level pointerover listener resolves `closest('[data-permanent-id], [data-portrait-target-player-id]')` (fast path) plus `elementsFromPoint` walk (slow path for tooltips/portals painted on top); arrows touching the hovered participant stay at opacity 1, others dim to 0.25 with a 120ms CSS opacity transition. **Structural progress:** combat-arrow code (CombatArrows + useCombatArrowGeometry + useCombatFingerprint + helpers) extracted from StackZone.tsx (was 865 LOC, well over the 500 hard cap with no documented exception) into new sibling `webclient/src/game/CombatArrows.tsx` (374 LOC). StackZone.tsx down to 655 LOC — still over the cap but with a documented-exception comment naming the next planned split (StackFan). **Critic findings caught (NOTABLE, all fixed pre-commit):** (1) `card.id ?? ''` was an over-defensive coercion that would collapse multiple arrows into one hover-isolation bucket if any permanent ever lacked an id — reverted to raw access since `card.id` is `z.string()` per webCardViewSchema. (2) Initial coordinate-based fan grouping (`Math.round(target/4)`) had a false-positive risk where two unrelated targets within 4px would fan together — switched to bucketing by canonical `arrow.targetId` (blocker permanent id or `player:<defenderId>`), which makes "same target" exact rather than approximate. (3) Initial pointerover-only hover hook would lose isolation when a tooltip or HoverCardDetail painted ON TOP of a combat tile because closest() on the tooltip element returned null — added `elementsFromPoint` slow path so the underlying tile is still resolved when the immediate hit isn't a combat element. (4) Documented-exception comment had self-contradictory LOC numbers between StackZone.tsx top-of-file and CombatArrows.tsx Javadoc — reconciled to 655. **Tests:** 5 new test cases in CombatArrows.test.tsx (single-arrow pass-through; 3-attacker fan distinctness + symmetry; full-opacity baseline; attacker-hover dims non-matching arrows; non-combat hover restores full opacity) + 2 new TargetingArrow.test.tsx cases for the new opacity prop (default 1; explicit forwards). Existing StackZone.test.tsx combat-mode block untouched and still green. 1230/1230 webclient tests green. **Tabletop load-bearing rules:** T1-T7 all preserved — arrows are an SVG overlay (T1), action panel unaffected (T2), no engine code (T5), no wire-format change (T5), 4-pod arrangement unaffected (T7). **Pre-existing tech debt acknowledged but out of scope:** TargetingArrow.tsx:54 has a `setState-in-effect` lint warning (pre-existing on the cursor-tracking code path I didn't touch — surfaced because lint ran on my touched files); StackZone.tsx framer-motion `transition: AnyTransition` typecheck errors at lines 153/161/418 (pre-existing in the StackFan rendering surface I didn't touch). |

| fix/scryfall-token-name-strip (2026-05-09, food/treasure/goblin tokens still 404 post-1.32 because engine name has " Token" suffix Scryfall doesn't recognize) | Mechanical (single-file webclient util fix + test updates; no wire change, no schema bump) | Builder only — diagnosis traced engine→wire→Scryfall, fix is a regex strip with self-evident shape | Technical (`covered by recon — engine constructors all stamp "X Token", probed Scryfall for 4 token sets confirming the bare-name URL works`), UI/UX (`no visual change beyond actual artwork now appearing — same as FB#13's rationale`), Magic-rules / Graphical / AI (`no rules / no motion / no AI surface`) | **Why this slice exists:** schema 1.32's named-lookup token URL path passed `cv.getName()` through verbatim, but every TokenImpl subclass (FoodToken, TreasureToken, GoblinToken, ServoToken, ...) calls `super("Food Token", ...)` / `super("Treasure Token", ...)` while Scryfall stores those tokens under bare names ("Food", "Treasure", "Goblin"). Result: `cards/named?exact=Food%20Token&set=teld` 404'd for the entire common-token case. **Fix:** `card.name.replace(/ Token$/, '')` before URL build in `webclient/src/game/scryfall.ts`; bare-named tokens (Avacyn, Angelo, Banana) pass through unchanged because the regex requires a leading space. **Verification probes:** `?exact=Food&set=teld` → 302; `?exact=Food%20Token&set=teld` → 404; `?exact=Food&set=tc21` → 302 (works even for empty-imageNumber DB rows so the strip restores the named-lookup path's set-coverage promise). **Critic-skip rationale (Mechanical tier per CLAUDE.md):** ≤50-LOC single-file source change + regex with explicit unit tests; no race / no architectural / no AI surface. Existing tests had been written with bare names ("Goblin") instead of engine names ("Goblin Token") — corrected to realistic inputs as part of the same edit so the regression-pin actually pins reality. **Tests:** updated 2 existing token-path tests to use engine names; added 3 new (Food regression-pin → exact=Food&set=teld, Avacyn no-strip case, bare "Token" degenerate case). 1245/1245 webclient tests green. **Out of scope (still deferred from 26fcc0c6 handoff):** XMAGE-namespace tokens (Copy/Morph/Manifest), Banana / un-databased tokens, sets without Scryfall token-set equivalents (e.g. tznc — `?exact=Treasure&set=tznc` 404s; Treasure is in tznr instead). **Tabletop rules:** N/A — universal client fix, T5's "no engine/wire/schema" applies to *variant work*, not utility-level token-URL fixes outside the variant-toggle scope. **Bundle:** dpl_GV1yxqsWkPLX67hcpST9tZ68i4k5 / index-DEz8vBUV.js live at modern-mage.com; commit a812d94f on origin/main. |

| INFRA-2 (2026-05-06, subdomain split: api.modern-mage.com for tunnel, modern-mage.com apex for Vercel) | Standard (single subdomain refactor; cloudflared-config.yml + Vercel custom domain + Cloudflare DNS edits; no Java code, no schema, no upstream impact) | Builder only — interactive user-driven slice; mid-execution Cloudflare/Vercel dashboard work | Technical (`covered by inline recon + live verification` — Phase A reversibility was the primary safeguard; recon caught both Cloudflare proxy gotchas; live end-to-end test before commit), UI/UX/Graphical (`no UI surface — pure ops`), Magic-rules (`no rules logic`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Two Cloudflare proxy gotchas surfaced live:** (1) When user moved apex DNS from tunnel-CNAME to Vercel A-records, they accidentally also flipped api.modern-mage.com's proxy state to gray cloud. Symptom: Test-NetConnection to the api hostname returned `fd10:aec2:5dae::` (private IPv6 ULA, RFC 4193 fd00::/8 range) and TCP failed; this is Cloudflare's response when a tunnel CNAME is "DNS only" — the cfargotunnel.com target is meaningless without Cloudflare's proxy. Fix: flip api back to orange cloud. (2) Conversely, the apex modern-mage.com MUST be "DNS only" (gray cloud) when pointed at Vercel — Cloudflare's docs and Vercel's docs both say so (double-proxy breaks Vercel's HTTP-01 SSL challenge + WebSocket reliability). The proxy-on-by-default UX makes this footgun easy to hit. **Two-phase rollout discipline:** Phase A (additive — add api ingress + DNS + Vercel env update + redeploy) was fully reversible: if anything broke, env reverts and we're back to INFRA-1's state. Phase B (cutover — Vercel custom domain + apex DNS swap) only ran after Phase A was verified live. End state: friends type `modern-mage.com` → Vercel React app; bundle calls `api.modern-mage.com` → Cloudflare-proxied tunnel → local JVM. All three URLs (modern-mage.com, api.modern-mage.com, xmage-playtest.vercel.app fallback) verified live pre-commit. **Reference memory updated:** `reference_cloudflare_tunnel_gotchas.md` extended with the orange-vs-gray decision matrix; `cloudflared-config.yml.template` comment block updated to call out the proxy-state requirement inline. **Critic-skip rationale:** `mage-stack` config edits + dashboard ops have no race surface beyond DNS propagation timing (visible via standard tools); no Java; no UI; no rules. Live verification (curl + bundle inspection) was the contract test. ADR 0013. |

| feat/pauper-format-test (T0 of Pauper roadmap, 2026-05-07) | Standard (test-only slice; new fixture file with 7 cases against upstream `mage.deck.Pauper` validator + `Constructed.legalRarity` rarity-walk via reflection; no production code, no pom changes, no webclient changes) | Builder, Technical critic, Magic-rules critic (parallel post-builder) | UI / UX / Graphical (`no UI surface — server-side test pinning runtime classpath + validator behavior; webclient PreLobbyModal already format-agnostic via serverState.deckTypes`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Why this slice:** user asked to add Pauper support; recon confirmed upstream `Mage.Server.Plugins/Mage.Deck.Constructed/src/mage/deck/Pauper.java` (commit a61c5f7, Apr 23 2026) already exists, auto-loads via DeckValidatorFactory at server startup, and webclient PreLobbyModal reads serverState.deckTypes dynamically — so "Constructed - Pauper" appears in our format dropdown on next deploy with zero production-code changes. This slice pins the contract via a test file so a future upstream rebase or pom shuffle can't silently kill the runtime classpath wiring. **Critic findings caught (zero blockers; 3 notables applied pre-commit):** (Tech-NOTABLE-1) brittle legal-deck test pointed at 6ED #61 directly — added `pauperValidator_acceptsBasicLandOnlyDeck` (60x Mountain) immune to printing-rarity drift. (MTG-NOTABLE) Counterspell at 6ED accidentally tested the "later common printing" code path but the test intent read as "Counterspell is common" — added `pauperValidator_acceptsCardViaLaterCommonPrinting` (4x Counterspell at 4ED #65 UNCOMMON + 56x Island) which explicitly pins the ever-common-anywhere rule, exercising `legalRarity`'s walk over `CardRepository.findCards(name)` across all printings rather than first-hit-wins. (Tech-NOTABLE-2) 4-of cap path uncovered — added `pauperValidator_rejectsOverFourCopies` (5x Lightning Bolt). **Critic findings deferred:** (Tech-NOTABLE) sideboard-banlist test queued as follow-up. (Tech-NIT-x3) `@TestInstance(PER_CLASS)` annotation redundant since `@BeforeAll` is static; `entry()` 1-line passthrough mild over-engineering; `cards.add(entry)` loop could be `Arrays.asList(entries)` — all queued for cleanup sweep. **MTG-rules per-card verification (Scryfall):** Lightning Bolt M11 #149 COMMON ✓; Counterspell 6ED #61 COMMON / 4ED #65 UNCOMMON ✓; Treasure Cruise KTK #59 COMMON + xmage banlist line 60 + real-world Pauper-banned ✓; Force of Will ALL #28 UNCOMMON, never-common across DMR / 2XM / EMA / VMA ✓. **Verification:** `mvn -f Mage.Server.WebApi/pom.xml test -Dtest=PauperFormatTest` → 7/7 green in 18.58s (CardScanner.scan loaded 89,222 cards / 574 sets / 2,454 tokens). **Next Pauper slice (T2):** Scryfall-backed legality service to replace the rarity-walk + 38-card static banlist (real banlist is ~74 cards as of Nov 2025; rarity walk also misses decreed exceptions like Hada Freeblade / Spatial Contortion that have promo-common printings but aren't Pauper-legal). |

| chore/pauper-roadmap-cleanup-pass (post-T4, 2026-05-07) | Mechanical (queued NITs from across T0/T2.A/T2.C/T4 critic logs; ≤200 LOC across 6 files; no architectural decisions, no API surface change, no behavior change) | Builder only — mechanical tier per CLAUDE.md cadence | Technical (`covered by builder + 29 Pauper Java tests + 1252 webclient tests passing — pure refactor + test-tightening`), Magic-rules / UI / UX / Graphical / AI (`no functional change`) | **Two commits, one cleanup pass:** `6e2f51a5` (Java side) + `2bf3c4e4` (webclient side). **Java side (commit 6e2f51a5):** (1) Extract `ScryfallManifest` from `ScryfallBulkDataClient`. The manifest-resolution logic (fetch `https://api.scryfall.com/bulk-data`, parse the `data` array, find the `oracle_cards` entry, return its `download_uri`) is logically distinct from the file-download + streaming-parse path. Pure refactor — `ScryfallBulkDataClient` 372 → 301 LOC (well under the 400 soft cap with room to grow); new `ScryfallManifest` 101 LOC. The `asInputStream` helper moves with the manifest path; `buildHttpErrorMessage` stays in `ScryfallBulkDataClient` as package-private static so both classes format errors with the same shape. No behavior change, no API surface change, validator behavior unchanged. (2) Drop redundant `@TestInstance(PER_CLASS)` from `PauperFormatTest` — the annotation isn't load-bearing because `@BeforeAll` is already static (works under `PER_METHOD` default identically). (3) Extract magic number `23` in `PauperLegalityServiceTest.loadsLegalitiesFromFixture` to a named `EXPECTED_DECK_LEGAL_COUNT` constant; the arithmetic comment ("30 entries total - 7 excluded = 23") now sits next to the constant declaration so the constant + derivation stay synced when the fixture changes. **Webclient side (commit 2bf3c4e4):** (1) `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"` on the format-info panel — screen-reader users navigating the format `<select>` now hear the description swap announced when they change formats; previously the swap was silent. (2) Tighten `PreLobbyModal` info-panel test to assert `target="_blank"` + `rel="noopener noreferrer"` on the banlist link. Previously the test only asserted `href`; a refactor that silently dropped `rel=noopener` (security hole — new tab could access window.opener) would have passed. Both attrs now pinned. **Critic findings deliberately deferred (NIT-tier, not worth the slice cost):** HashMap initial capacity tuning in `ScryfallBulkDataClient` (micro-optimization, no measurable benefit); `cacheDir` field unread post-construction in `LegalityCacheStore` (cosmetic); `entry()` 1-line passthrough helper in `PauperFormatTest` (coin-flip readability, the helper IS marginally clearer); `cards.add(entry)` loop → `Arrays.asList()` (same); anonymous test-subclass passing real `HttpClient.newHttpClient()` to a constructor that overrides the only method using it (cosmetic); info panel uses same `bg-elevated` token as parent modal (designer-call visual polish); `-mt-3` couples to parent `gap-5` flow (works fine, refactor is structural). **Verification:** Java `mvn -f Mage.Server.WebApi/pom.xml test -Dtest='WebApiPauperValidatorTest,PauperLegalityServiceTest,PauperFormatTest'` → 29/29 green. Webclient `pnpm test --run` → 1252/1252 green. **End of vanilla-Pauper roadmap cleanup.** Pauper user-facing functionality + operational polish + UX surface + cleanup all SHIPPED 2026-05-07. T3 (Pauper Commander / PDH) deferred indefinitely as a separate format. Display-card / player-portrait followup unblocked. |

| feat/pauper-format-info-panel (T4 of Pauper roadmap, 2026-05-07) | Standard (single-component feature; new util file + 1-component edit + tests; webclient-only — no schema bump, no Java, no wire change) | Builder + combined Technical/UI critic (post-builder) | Magic-rules (`no fixture changes — static descriptive copy only`), UX (`covered by UI critic — panel placement + a11y are UI-tier concerns`), Graphical (`no motion`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Why this slice:** last functional slice of the Pauper roadmap, paired with the user's constraint "current lobby is amazing on design basis — no graphical changes" — surfaces format meaning + banlist link without restructuring the modal. Considered server-side metadata (DTO field on `WebDeckType`) but rejected as overengineering for static cosmetic copy + an external link; webclient-only static map is the right scope. Future slice can promote to a wire field if we ever want server-controlled copy or surface the info on other UI surfaces (deck import flow, replay viewer). **Architecture:** new `formatInfo.ts` exports `Readonly<Record<string, FormatInfo>>` keyed by deckType string (`Constructed - Pauper`, `Commander`, etc.) with `{ description: string; banlistUrl: string \| null }` values. `getFormatInfo(deckType)` returns `FormatInfo \| null` — unknown formats (custom plugins, future upstream additions we haven't curated) return null and the info panel simply doesn't render. 8 formats included, all string keys verified against the exact `super("...")` argument in the corresponding upstream class file (Pauper.java:14, Standard.java:18, Pioneer.java:17, Modern.java:17, Legacy.java:13, Vintage.java:13, Commander.java:14-15, Limited.java:16). Render block in PreLobbyModal sits between the existing Format and Mode `<Field>` blocks with `-mt-3` negative margin to pull under the dropdown without disturbing the parent's `gap-5` flow. Banlist link uses `target="_blank"` + `rel="noopener noreferrer"` for security. `useMemo` keyed on `deckType` only (lookup is into a module-level constant). **Critic findings caught + applied pre-commit:** (UI-NOTABLE-N1) decorative `↗` arrow on the banlist link wrapped in `<span aria-hidden="true">` so screen readers announce "View banlist" instead of "View banlist north east arrow". **Critic findings deferred (queued for NIT cleanup pass):** (UI-NOTABLE-N2) no `aria-live` on the panel — keyboard / screen-reader users navigating the format `<select>` don't get the description-swap announced. (UI-NIT) info panel reuses the same `--color-bg-elevated` token as the parent modal; only the border distinguishes them — slight visual blend. (UI-NIT) `-mt-3` couples to parent `gap-5` — if the modal's gap is ever changed, the panel visually binds; could move to nested `<Field>` wrapper as a structural fix. (UI-NIT) test asserts `href` but not `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"` — a future refactor could quietly drop `noopener` and the test would still pass. (Theme NIT, builder-flagged) `text-text-link` doesn't exist in the theme; fell back to `text-status-info` (`#5BC0F0`, token-backed) instead of inventing a literal Tailwind blue. **Pre-existing typecheck errors NOT introduced by this slice:** the typecheck failure at `PreLobbyModal.test.tsx:24` is rot in the existing test fixture (missing `registrationEnabled` field added in slice F18); T4 added a new test ~34 lines at the bottom of the file but didn't touch line 24. Other pre-existing errors in `Battlefield.tsx`, `App.tsx:290`, `BattlefieldRowGroup`, `battlefieldRows.test.ts`, `useModalA11y.test.tsx`, etc. are also independent of this slice. **Verification:** `pnpm test --run` → 1252/1252 green (4 new in `formatInfo.test.ts`, 1 new in `PreLobbyModal.test.tsx`). **DTO firewall:** zero `mage.view.*` references (webclient doesn't have access to those types anyway). **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop variant rules (T1-T7):** N/A — slice is in `PreLobbyModal` which is the pre-table-create flow, not part of the tabletop game-table layout. **End of T4 + end of vanilla-Pauper roadmap.** Remaining queued: NIT cleanup pass (collapse the deferred items above + queued T0/T2.A NITs from earlier rows). T3 (Pauper Commander / PDH) is a separate-format multi-slice arc, deferred indefinitely. The display-card / player-portrait followup unblocks for whenever the user wants it. |

| feat/pauper-legality-scheduled-refresh (T2.C of Pauper roadmap, 2026-05-07) | Standard (existing service re-architected to atomic-snapshot pattern; new daemon `ScheduledExecutorService` with named thread; `AutoCloseable` lifecycle wired into `WebApiServer.stop()`; new env var `XMAGE_LEGALITY_REFRESH_INTERVAL_HOURS`) | Builder + Technical critic (post-builder, against the diff) | Magic-rules (`no fixture changes — operational behavior only`), UI / UX / Graphical (`no UI surface`), AI (`no AI surface`) | **Why this slice:** T2.A+B left vanilla Pauper functionally complete, but the banlist update path required `mage-redeploy` to force a Scryfall re-fetch. Wizards' Pauper Format Panel typically announces bans on Mondays; without a daily refresh the operator has to remember to redeploy on every B&R announcement or serve a stale snapshot. T2.C adds a daemon refresh executor (default 24h) so the banlist tracks within ≤24h automatically. **Architecture changes:** (1) `final Map` + `final Instant` → `AtomicReference<LegalitySnapshot>` holding a `(Map, Instant)` pair so refresh can swap both atomically — readers can never see a torn (new map, old timestamp) pair. (2) `refreshOnce()` catches `Throwable` (not just `IOException`) — `ScheduledExecutorService` SUPPRESSES future ticks if a runnable throws, and a permanently-dead refresh thread would be a silent operational regression. (3) Implements `AutoCloseable`; `WebApiServer.stop()` chains `legalityService.close()` in a nested try-finally with the existing Javalin `app.stop` + `authService.close` teardown so failures on one path don't leak the others. (4) `withLegalityCacheDir` made idempotent — second invocation closes the prior service first (defensive; canonical WebApiMain path calls it exactly once). **Production constructor signature change:** single-arg `PauperLegalityService(Path)` removed in favor of `PauperLegalityService(Path cacheDir, Duration refreshInterval)` to force explicit interval choice at the call site. 3-arg test-seam constructor preserved as a `Duration.ZERO`-delegating backwards-compat overload so T2.B's `WebApiPauperValidatorTest` continues compiling unchanged. **Critic findings caught + applied pre-commit:** (Tech-NOTABLE-N1, Javadoc-reorder) the test-seam Javadoc was positioned above the 3-arg backwards-compat overload, leaving the 4-arg test-seam constructor with no immediately-preceding doc; reordered. (Tech-NOTABLE-N2, idempotent guard) `withLegalityCacheDir` would have leaked a daemon thread if called twice mid-process; added a "close prior + null + log WARN" guard. **Mid-pass test failure caught + fixed:** initial implementation used `refreshInterval.getSeconds()` + `TimeUnit.SECONDS` to schedule, which truncated `Duration.ofMillis(50)` (used by the `close_stopsScheduledExecutor` test) to 0 → `scheduleAtFixedRate` threw `IllegalArgumentException` ("period must be positive") at boot. Switched to `toMillis()` + `TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS` so any positive Duration is supported (production 24h case is unaffected). **Critic findings deferred:** (Tech-NIT-Nit2) `refreshOnce` could theoretically race on the cache file if a test invoked it concurrently with the scheduler — current usage doesn't expose that risk (tests use `Duration.ZERO`); flagged for revisit if any test ever runs the scheduler. **Verification:** `mvn -f Mage.Server.WebApi/pom.xml test -Dtest='WebApiPauperValidatorTest,PauperLegalityServiceTest,PauperFormatTest'` → 29/29 green (T0 7, T2.A+C 11, T2.B 11). PauperLegalityService.java grew 184→326 LOC (still under 400 soft cap). **DTO firewall:** zero `mage.view.*` references. **Schema impact:** none — purely operational. **Upstream rebase impact:** none — runtime behavior change in our module only. **Concurrency contract:** `legalityOf` reads `AtomicReference.get()` once per call → returns immutable map snapshot → safe with concurrent atomic swaps from the single-thread refresh executor; no locking needed for readers. **WebApiPauperValidator unchanged** — captures `legalityService` reference once in constructor; per-validation reads route through the volatile `AtomicReference`, so swap is transparent. **Next slice (T4):** format-info UX in `PreLobbyModal` — schema bump (`description` + `banlistUrl` on `WebDeckType`), zod + CHANGELOG migration note + tooltip render. Cross-stack but small. **End of T2 arc; vanilla Pauper is functionally + operationally complete.** |

(2) SID-match regex in 3 sites switched from bare `[regex]::Escape($sid)` to `;[regex]::Escape($sid)\)` — anchors the SID to the ACE-terminator position. **Mid-fix iteration caught:** first attempt at the SID anchor used a literal `)` in the regex pattern; PowerShell's regex engine threw `Too many )'s` ("`)` is a group-close metacharacter"). Live `Test-AdminOrAclGrant` probe immediately after the helper edit caught it before commit. Fixed by escaping the `)` to `\)` in the pattern string. **Verification:** post-fix `Test-AdminOrAclGrant -ServiceName MageWebApi/MageTunnel/MageWatchdog` returns `True` (granted services correctly detected); `NonExistent` returns `False`; `$LASTEXITCODE` clean. Tier 2 — same observable behavior today (no other ACEs on these services contain an overlapping SID), but the defensive correctness covers the silent-failure edge case the audit flagged. **Critic-skip rationale (Trivial tier per CLAUDE.md):** the matrix explicitly says "Trivial — applying critic's own findings, doc typos, single-line fix → builder only, no critic".
