Slot 0 (above the BEGIN_COMBAT dot) carries the phase header text "Combat" instead of "Begin" — the user explicitly authorized this trade-off ("If the first label overlaps with combat, just remove the first label (which I assume is 'Begin Combat'?)"). Priority status suffix (`PRIORITY` / `PASSING` / `WAITING · name`) now attaches to whichever slot is the active sub-step (the orb-bearing label) rather than always to the "Combat" header text — this keeps the suffix where the user's attention is. **Mechanism:** added `isCombatCompactRunway = compact && phase.label === 'Combat'` flag; when true, the centered-phase-header `<div>` is replaced by 6 absolute-positioned `<span>`s (one per `phase.steps[idx]`) at `left: (idx + 0.5) / 6 * 100%` of segment width with `-translate-x-1/2`. Slot 0 (`idx === 0`) renders `phase.label` ("Combat") instead of `step.short` ("Begin"); slot 0 visual styling is overridden to follow phase-active status (red+bold when `isActivePhase`, dim otherwise) regardless of sub-step `position`, and slot 0 never carries the ✓ check-mark even when `position === 'past'` (the phase header isn't a sub-step that gets ticked off). New `data-combat-header="true"` attribute on slot 0 lets consumers distinguish the phase header from sub-step stickers without breaking the existing `data-step="BEGIN_COMBAT"` anchor. Below-dots labels-row is suppressed for the combat segment in compact mode via `showBelowDotsSubStepLabels && !isCombatCompactRunway`. **Non-compact variant (`?variant=current`) preserves exact today's behavior** — the legacy centered phase header + below-dots sub-step labels render unchanged for every phase. **Off-screen bleed analysis:** combat segment is `6/13` of the timeline width; at the 1440p T4 target with a ~1300px timeline → combat ≈ 600px → per-slot width ≈ 100px; longest sub-step label is `1st Strike` (~50-65px at `text-[9px]`); all labels fit within their slot widths without bleeding past segment boundaries; slot 0 ("Combat") + priority suffix when player names are short (typical "alice" / "guest-abc123" / Discord-handle names) stays inside the slot; long player names may bleed into slot 1 — accepted tradeoff per the existing tooltip-scale slider's "can block other elements" framing. **Test contract migration (4 tests updated, 18 unchanged):** (1) "omits sub-step labels for every phase when active phase is NOT combat" → renamed to "combat-only — 6 sub-step labels are permanent in compact, non-combat phases stay suppressed" + asserts 1 `phase-step-labels` container with `data-combat-runway="permanent"` + 6 `phase-step-label`s with slot 0 carrying `data-combat-header="true"` + text "Combat". (2) "omits sub-step labels in beginning, main, and end phases" updated to assert the combat segment's permanent runway is present while other segments still suppress. (3) "marks past sub-steps as past and renders a check-mark sigil" — `phase-step-past-mark` count drops `4 → 3` because slot 0 (BEGIN_COMBAT phase-header slot) suppresses the ✓ even though its `data-step-position="past"` still reflects sub-step lifecycle. (4) "exposes data-combat-active on the combat segment + runway when active" — switches the active runway assertion from `data-combat-runway === 'true'` to a pair: `data-combat-runway === 'permanent'` (always for combat in compact) AND `data-combat-runway-active === 'true'` (only when the combat phase is active). **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none — pure webclient render change. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** T1 (zones fixed) — timeline is top-of-viewport, not pod-internal; unaffected. T2 (action panel floats) — unaffected. T3 (full Scryfall art) — unaffected. T4 (1440p) — primary target; bleed analysis above confirms fit. T5 (no engine code, no wire change, no schemaVersion bump) — respected. T6 (tabletop default = the surface modified) — respected. T7 (cross/plus 4-pod) — unaffected. **Spec rigor (ADR 0014):** D1 paraphrase block written in conversation pre-build + AskUserQuestion preview prompted explicit user confirmation of the design before any code touched (mechanical-tier exemption from the paraphrase block doesn't apply because this slice DID redesign visible chrome, but the user-confirm step substitutes the equivalent verification). D2 acceptance criteria all countable (combat segment renders 6 `phase-step-label`s in compact mode regardless of `activePhase`; slot 0 carries `data-combat-header="true"` + text "Combat"; slot 0 suppresses the ✓ mark; phase-timeline height stays constant; non-compact variant unchanged; other 4 phase segments unchanged; full suite stays green except 2 pre-existing baselines). **Test gate:** typecheck clean; 22/22 across PhaseTimeline (was 22/22 before — 4 tests updated in place, no net new tests); full suite 1746/1748 (unchanged from previous slice — same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog + PlayerPortrait baselines unrelated). |
| UX — phase ladder text universal cream-white for readability (2026-05-11) | Mechanical (single-file find/replace of every text-color Tailwind class in `PhaseTimeline.tsx` → `text-amber-50`; no DOM-shape change, no layout change, no behavior change beyond text color). | Builder ONLY | All critics skipped per CLAUDE.md mechanical-tier — `pre-coding 12-bullet enumeration of every text field in the ladder, presented to the user for explicit confirmation (AskUserQuestion + a follow-up "tell me every text field this applies to" pre-commit gate). User confirmed strictly-literal "all text → cream white" interpretation; the trade-off (loss of phase-accent color on labels in exchange for uniform readability, with active state still cued by font-semibold + colored orb glow + colored dots + colored track bar) was explicitly accepted. No motion / a11y / engine / wire / schema surface — pure Tailwind class substitution.` | **Trigger:** user-reported readability issue with the dim text colors (`text-zinc-500` / `text-zinc-600` / `text-zinc-400`) on the `bg-zinc-950` near-black background of the phase ladder. After enumerating every text field in the surface (12 fields across phase headers, sub-step labels, slot 0 combat header, priority suffix words, waiting-on player name, Turn N, active player name in non-compact left block) and presenting the "strictly literal vs preserve accent colors" tradeoff via AskUserQuestion, user chose strictly literal: all text becomes `text-amber-50` (Tailwind warm cream `#fffbeb`), active state cued by `font-semibold` only. **Mechanism:** 10 sequential `replace_all`s on `PhaseTimeline.tsx` — `text-cyan-300` / `text-sky-300` / `text-red-300` / `text-emerald-300` / `text-purple-300` (5 `fgClass` values per phase) / `text-zinc-200` (active player name) / `text-zinc-400` (priority passing/waiting suffix + waiting-on name) / `text-zinc-500` (Turn N + future sub-step labels) / `text-zinc-600` (past sub-step + inactive phase header) / `text-amber-300` (PRIORITY callout) → all become `text-amber-50`. 16 lines touched across 4 logical regions (TIMELINE_PHASES config / non-compact left block / PhaseSegment label rendering / PriorityStatusSuffix). **Active-state differentiation preserved via:** (a) `font-semibold` on active phase / step / priority labels (was always there, now load-bearing); (b) the active orb's phase-colored glow + fill (unchanged); (c) each phase's dot color + track bar color (unchanged — `bg-cyan-400` / `bg-sky-400` / `bg-red-400` / `bg-emerald-400` / `bg-purple-400` etc. are all background colors on the dots, not text). So phase identity still reads from the dots/track even though labels are uniform cream. **What's NOT changed:** dots (`bg-*-400`), track bars (`bg-*-500/70`), active orb glow (`rgba(${phase.glowRgb}, ...)` box-shadow), bullet separator opacity (still inherits cream-white at 50% opacity), DOM structure, font-size classes, layout / spacing. **Test contract:** existing `expect(suffix.innerHTML).toContain('text-amber-300')` assertion at PhaseTimeline.test.tsx:265 updated to assert `text-amber-50` + `font-semibold` (the contract shifts from "color is amber-300" to "color is uniform amber-50 + active state has font-semibold"). All other 21 PhaseTimeline tests pass unchanged because they don't assert on color classes. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** pure text-color styling on existing top header; T1 (zones unaffected), T2 (action panel unaffected), T3 (cards unaffected), T4 (1440p target), T5 (no engine code / no wire change / no schema bump), T6 (tabletop default surface), T7 (4-pod unaffected). **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Spec rigor (ADR 0014):** D1 paraphrase block written pre-build with explicit user confirmation of the literal interpretation; D2 acceptance criteria all countable (zero `text-zinc-*` / `text-cyan-*` / `text-sky-*` / `text-red-*` / `text-emerald-*` / `text-purple-*` / `text-amber-3*` remaining in `PhaseTimeline.tsx`; 16 `text-amber-50` instances; full test suite stays at 1746/1748). **Test gate:** typecheck clean; 22/22 PhaseTimeline; full suite 1746/1748 (unchanged — same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog + PlayerPortrait baselines unrelated). |
| Tests — Scry/Surveil Skip contract pair (webclient SelectDialog + WebApi helper) (2026-05-11) | Mechanical (additive tests + small extract-method refactor on the WebApi side; no production behavior change. Pins both sides of the wire contract that was load-bearing for the 2026-05-11 scry/surveil Skip bug fix: webclient encodes "skip" as the all-zeros UUID sentinel; WebApi translates it back to a Java null before invoking the engine.) | Builder ONLY | All critics skipped per CLAUDE.md mechanical-tier — `additive test coverage + a 3-line extract-method refactor. No DOM-shape, no schema, no DTO, no engine, no wire change. The refactor's only behavior visibility is "static helper exists and is unit-tested"; the existing 51-test GameStreamHandlerTest integration suite (which exercises the exact case-uuid dispatch path) is the safety net that catches any regression in the refactor itself.` | **Trigger:** post-2026-05-11 fix (commit `5841604dfe` shipped the all-zeros → null translation that unblocked scry/surveil Skip), the user asked "can we somehow create a test that guarantees the Scry and Surveil modal is working?" The existing single test (`playerResponse_uuidKind_allZerosSkipSentinel_doesNotError`) only asserted "dispatch doesn't error" — a weak contract that would still pass if the all-zeros-to-null translation were silently removed. No webclient-side test existed for SelectDialog at all (an entire surface — the multi-pick dialog for tutors / scry partition / surveil partition / discard / reveal — was untested at the component level). Both gaps are the contract this slice closes. **Mechanism (WebApi side — extract + unit test):** the `case "uuid"` branch in `handlePlayerResponse` previously inlined `SKIP_SENTINEL_UUID_TEXT.equals(uuidText) ? null : UUID.fromString(uuidText)`. Extracted to a new package-private static helper `GameStreamHandler.parsePlayerResponseUuidOrSkip(String) → UUID` (returns `null` for the sentinel, parsed UUID otherwise, throws IllegalArgumentException for malformed input). Three new unit tests in `GameStreamHandlerDecodeTest` (the existing pure-unit JUnit home for static helpers on `GameStreamHandler`) pin the contract: (1) all-zeros sentinel → null; (2) real UUID → parsed UUID; (3) malformed → IllegalArgumentException. **Mechanism (webclient side — new SelectDialog.test.tsx):** new test file `webclient/src/game/dialogs/SelectDialog.test.tsx` (~270 LOC, 7 tests). Mirrors the established pattern in `TargetDialog.test.tsx` — spy on `GameStream.prototype.sendPlayerResponse`, render `SelectDialog` directly with hand-crafted dialog payloads, exercise the Skip / submit affordances. Five dialog fixtures: **Scry shape** (min=0, max=2, flag=true, message verbatim from `PlayerImpl.java:5471-5473` — `"card or cards to PUT on the BOTTOM of your library (Scry)"`); **Surveil shape** (min=0, max=3, flag=true, message verbatim from `PlayerImpl.java:5500-5502` — `"card or cards to PUT into your GRAVEYARD (Surveil)"`); **Single-pick** (min=max=1, flag=true, tutor-style); **Optional single-pick** (min=0, max=1, flag=false). **Coverage:** (1+2) Scry + Surveil shapes render the Skip button; (3+4) clicking Skip on each sends `(messageId, 'uuid', '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000')` to the stream + calls `clearDialog`; (5) mandatory single-pick has NO Skip button (regression guard for `allowSkip = !flag \|\| min === 0`); (6) clicking a card in single-pick mode sends THAT card's UUID, NOT the all-zeros sentinel (regression guard); (7) optional shape (flag=false) also sends the sentinel. **What the contract pair guarantees:** if the webclient ever changes its Skip encoding (e.g., switches to a different sentinel string, switches to a separate `kind:"skip"` message, or drops the Skip path entirely), test (3+4+7) fails. If the WebApi ever stops translating the all-zeros sentinel to null (e.g., a refactor removes the special-case), the helper unit test fails. The engine's null-handling for `chooseTarget(Cards)` is upstream and stable — covered by upstream's own Mage.Tests harness for the scry / surveil paths in `PlayerImpl.scry()` / `PlayerImpl.doSurveil()`. Together: the two sides of the wire are pinned; the engine's null-terminator semantics are trusted. **Engine message strings copied verbatim from `PlayerImpl.java`** (engine-emitted strings, not idealized stand-ins) per the lesson recorded in `feedback_engine_emitted_test_fixtures.md` — when the engine source is the ground truth, the test fixture should grep / copy that source rather than guess. **What's NOT changed:** behavior of the case-uuid dispatch is identical (same null fed to `sendPlayerUUID` for the sentinel, same UUID fed for real values, same BAD_REQUEST emitted for malformed) — verified by all 51 existing GameStreamHandlerTest cases still passing. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none — pure additions in our own modules. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — testing infrastructure only; no DOM/layout/engine surface. **Spec rigor (ADR 0014):** D1 paraphrase block written in conversation pre-build + AskUserQuestion preview confirmed "both contract tests" interpretation before any code touched. D2 acceptance criteria all countable (3 new Java unit tests pass; 7 new webclient tests pass; existing 12-test GameStreamHandlerDecodeTest stays at 12+3=15; existing 51-test GameStreamHandlerTest stays at 51; webclient full suite delta +7). **Test gate:** Java side 15/15 DecodeTest + 51/51 handler integration; webclient typecheck clean + 7/7 SelectDialog tests + full suite 1753/1755 (was 1746/1748; +7 new tests; same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog + PlayerPortrait baselines unrelated). |
| Slice REJOIN-A — "Rejoin Game" lobby surface for disconnected-but-seated players (2026-05-10) | Standard (new authenticated REST endpoint + new server service file + new client banner component + new client polling hook + extra hand-written DTOs + cross-stack thread-through from `App.tsx` → `Lobby.tsx` → banner. Engine state read via existing `GameLookup` reflection helpers — no upstream-file modification. Banner polls at the existing 5s tables-list cadence. Empty-list common case ⇒ banner returns null.) | Technical + UX (parallel dispatch from scope brief) + Magic-rules (retroactive, narrowly scoped to the rejoinability predicate — user-prompted re-examination caught that "is this user still in this game" is actually a load-bearing rules call); Graphical SKIP — `no motion / no animation surface; banner is a static section that fades in only via the natural browser paint when poll #N first surfaces a game; no Framer / CSS-keyframe involvement`. | **Mechanism (server, 3 new files + 1 edit):** new DTOs `WebMyGame` (`gameId`, `tableId?`, `tableName`, `opponentNames[]`) and `WebMyGamesResponse` (`schemaVersion`, `games[]`) — hand-written records, no `mage.view.*` leakage. New `GameMembershipService` (~180 LOC) walks `gameManager().getGameController()` (the public accessor on `GameManagerImpl:195`); for each controller resolves the user's seat via `GameLookup.findUserPlayerMap()`, the engine `Game` via `GameLookup.findGame()`, then reads `Player.hasLeft()` and `Player.getName()` directly off `mage.players.Player` (existing precedent — `AuthService.connectionStateFor` does the same at `AuthService.java:330-367`). Opponent names exclude the rejoining user; table name resolved via `tableManager().getTable(tableId).getName()`. Service is fail-closed on every branch (null session, unresolvable upstream userId, reflection-poisoned, table removed) — returns empty response, banner stays hidden. New route `GET /api/games/mine` registered alongside existing `/api/server/main-room` (line ~611 of `WebApiServer.java`) — auth-gated through the same `BearerAuthMiddleware → sessionFrom(ctx)` pattern as every other protected endpoint. Three new integration tests in `WebApiServerTest.java`: empty-list-when-no-games (happy path + DTO shape pin), no-token returns 401, invalid-token returns 401. **Mechanism (client, 2 new files + 3 edits):** new Zod schemas `webMyGameSchema` + `webMyGamesResponseSchema` in `api/schemas.ts` (mirror server DTO; `tableId` nullable per server emit shape). New `useRejoinPoll` hook (~80 LOC, `webclient/src/lobby/useRejoinPoll.ts`) owns the 5s GET poll on `/api/games/mine`; fail-closed reset to `[]` on any error (rejoin surface is enhancement, not a feature whose failure should surface a red error string). New `RejoinGameBanner` component (~100 LOC) renders an `<section aria-label="Games you can rejoin" aria-live="polite">` with per-row "Rejoin <tableName>" buttons. `Lobby.tsx` accepts a new `onRejoinGame?(gameId)` prop, calls `useRejoinPoll`, mounts the banner above the existing "Tables" header. `App.tsx` threads `setActiveGameId` as the click handler — same setter the start-game auto-route uses, so reload-resume / fresh-start-resume / rejoin-resume all converge on one code path. **Critic findings + fixes applied:** **TECHNICAL B1 (BLOCKER, applied):** `Lobby.tsx` reached 501 LOC, 1 over the 500 hard cap, when the rejoin fetch effect was inlined. Extracted the state + effect to `useRejoinPoll` per the critic's recommended split; `Lobby.tsx` now sits at 460 LOC. **UX B1 (BLOCKER, applied):** initial palette used amber-500 (border/fill/CTA), colliding with the `text-amber-400` color the lobby already uses for `READY_TO_START` table state (`Lobby.tsx:492`) — would create two amber zones competing for "act here" attention. Switched the banner family to emerald (border `emerald-500/40`, fill `emerald-500/5`, header `emerald-200`, icon `emerald-300`, CTA `bg-emerald-500 text-zinc-950`). Emerald reads as "alive — come back" and is otherwise unused in the lobby, so the rejoin signal stays distinct. **TECHNICAL N1 (NOTABLE, applied):** `Game.getPlayers()` returns a non-concurrent `LinkedHashMap` (per `Mage/.../Players.java`); a full `entrySet()` walk during a concurrent `Game.addPlayer` could throw CME. Wrapped both `game.getPlayer(myPlayerId)` and `collectOpponentNames()` in try-catch on `RuntimeException` → return empty for that game, banner stays hidden for one poll cycle. **TECHNICAL N4 / UX N2 (NOTABLE, applied):** opponent list could overflow at 4-player FFA. Added `formatOpponents(names)` helper — ≤2 names render verbatim ("vs alice, bob"), 3+ collapse to "first +N more" ("vs alice +3 more") so the line stays scannable on narrow viewports and SR users hear a count instead of listening through a comma list. **UX N4 (NOTABLE, applied):** added `aria-live="polite"` on the banner section so SR users mid-session hear announcement when poll #N first surfaces a new rejoinable game. **UX Ni4 (NIT, applied):** per-button `aria-label="Rejoin <tableName>"` so SR users iterating multiple rows hear distinct labels instead of "Rejoin button, Rejoin button." **TECHNICAL T1+T2+T3 (NITs, applied):** added `import mage.webapi.lobby.GameMembershipService;` instead of fully-qualifying, blank-line separator above the new route registration, stashed `controller.getTableId()` in a local instead of calling twice. **MAGIC-RULES N1 (NOTABLE, applied — retroactive critic):** the initial filter on `Player.hasLeft()` was the **right rule, wrong field**. `hasLeft()=true` only fires under CR 800.4a "leaves the game" (`PlayerImpl.leave()` at `PlayerImpl.java:2718-2727`) plus the pre-start join-punt — it does NOT fire on mid-game concede (`PlayerImpl.concede()` sets `loses=true` but not `left=true`), response-idle timeout, timer timeout, or "loses the game" effects (life=0 / deck-out / poison / -1/-1 counters). Pre-fix, a 4-player game where opponent A died on turn 6 would still advertise that game as rejoinable to A (and A would stay listed in pod-mates' opponent rosters forever). The engine's canonical predicate is `Player.isInGame()` (`PlayerImpl.java:2829-2831`), composing `!hasQuit() && !hasLost() && !hasWon() && !hasDrew() && !hasLeft()`. The upstream `Player.java:289` even has an explicit TODO recommending this substitution. Changed the filter from `me.hasLeft()` to `!me.isInGame()` (one-line code change + Javadoc update on the class explaining the predicate choice). Strict superset — every previously-filtered player stays filtered, and dead/conceded/timed-out players are now correctly excluded too. No regression possible; the 3 existing server tests still pass (they exercise the empty-list / auth-gate paths, none of which touched this branch). **DEFERRED (NOTABLES that need bigger scope):** TECHNICAL N2 — "user IS in a game" integration test deferred because standing up a live engine game in the test harness (deck submission + AI seats + waiting for match start) is brittle and slow; positive path covered by 4 of the 9 client component tests using mocked API responses, and the service's filter logic will exercise on live deploys; recommend live smoke-test post-merge. TECHNICAL N3 — emitting `null` vs `""` for `tableId` is bikeshed; current shape is fine and Zod schema is `.nullable()`. UX N5 — most-recently-disconnected ordering needs a server-side timestamp field (queue as a future slice; current iteration order is fine for the 1-game case which is 99% of users). **DTO firewall:** verified — `WebMyGame` / `WebMyGamesResponse` are records of primitives + `List<String>`; no `mage.view.*` anywhere in public surface. Internal reads of `mage.players.Player` / `mage.game.Game` are existing precedent. **Schema impact:** none. New endpoint is purely additive; no `schemaVersion` bump on existing wire. New DTO stamps `SchemaVersion.CURRENT` (1.35) for forward-compat per the REST-endpoint convention. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. Zero edits in `Mage/`, `Mage.Server/`, `Mage.Common/`, `Mage.Sets/`, root `pom.xml`, `Makefile`. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — lobby surface, not the in-game tabletop variant. **File LOC discipline:** all new files well under 500 LOC; `Lobby.tsx` went 436 → 501 → 460 after the hook extraction; `WebApiServer.java` ~ +7 LOC for field + ctor init + route; `WebApiServerTest.java` test-file-exempt. **Spec rigor (ADR 0014):** D1 paraphrase block written in scope brief pre-build with explicit user confirmation via AskUserQuestion (banner-above-tables placement + tableName + opponents-only content). D2 acceptance criteria all countable (`GET /api/games/mine` returns `WebMyGamesResponse` with `schemaVersion` + `games[]`; 3 server tests pass; 9 client tests pass + 60 lobby/app tests no-regression; reflection failures return empty list not 500; banner returns null on empty; banner uses emerald palette not amber; aria-live="polite" present; per-row aria-label present). **Test gate:** Java side 3/3 new + WebApi clean test-compile; client typecheck clean + 9/9 RejoinGameBanner + 60/60 Lobby+App; full lobby suite passes; no new lint findings in any touched file (52 pre-existing lint problems elsewhere in the codebase are unrelated). |
| Slice REJOIN-B — positive-path integration test for `GET /api/games/mine` (2026-05-11) | Standard (single new `@Test` method in `WebApiServerTest.java` exercising the REJOIN-A surface end-to-end via the existing host+AI+join+start helpers; no production-code change, no wire/schema/DTO change, no upstream-file touch; risk surface limited to the async timing window between `/start` returning 204 and `GameManager.getGameController()` containing the new GameController). | Technical (single agent dispatched on the scope brief). UX SKIP — `no user-visible surface; test-only file`. Magic-rules SKIP — `isInGame() correctness was already settled in REJOIN-A's Magic-rules pass; this slice only verifies the wiring around it via HTTP`. Graphical SKIP — `no motion, no DOM, no animation`. | **Mechanism:** one new `@Test getGamesMine_userInLiveGame_returnsThatGame` (~70 LOC including the field-count assert + 5s poll loop) added to `WebApiServerTest.java` directly below the existing 3 negative-path rejoin tests. Reuses the exact same helper sequence as the existing `endToEnd_createTableAddAiJoinStart_advancesTableState` (`freshAnonBearer` → `/api/session/me` → `mainRoomId` → `createTableWith` `Two Player Duel` `Constructed - Vintage` `["HUMAN","COMPUTER_MONTE_CARLO"]` → `/api/rooms/{room}/tables/{table}/ai` → `buildForestDeckJson(60)` → `/join` → `/start`). After `/start` returns 204, polls `GET /api/games/mine` on a 100ms cadence for up to 5s, filters returned games by THIS test's tableId, and asserts on the matching entry. **Pin contract:** (1) `myEntry.size() == 4` — locks the 4-field `WebMyGame` wire shape (`gameId`, `tableId`, `tableName`, `opponentNames`); mirrors the established `assertEquals(19, table.size(), ...)` precedent at the same file's createTable test (line ~593) so a future additive field on `WebMyGame` without a `schemaVersion` bump fails-loud here. (2) `gameId` is present, non-blank, distinct from `tableId` (the engine Game UUID is NOT the table UUID — both concepts appear on the wire). (3) `tableName` is non-null (`""` allowed — service emits empty string when table lookup fails per `resolveTableName` at `GameMembershipService.java:192-202`). (4) `opponentNames.size() == 1` and the entry is non-blank + not equal to the host's username — pins the "exclude requesting user" semantics + the "AI seat is included" semantics without hardcoding the bot name (which varies by playerType across upstream builds). (5) Response `schemaVersion == SchemaVersion.CURRENT` — same pin the empty-list test enforces. **Why the 5s poll cadence:** engine game-start is async — `MageServerImpl.startMatch` returns 204 from the HTTP handler before the worker thread finishes constructing the `Game` + registering its `GameController` in the JVM-singleton `GameManager.getGameController()` map. The 5s deadline + 100ms inter-poll sleep deliberately mirrors the live lobby's `useRejoinPoll` cadence (`webclient/src/lobby/useRejoinPoll.ts`) so the test exercises the same observability shape a real player would see. Typical in-process latency observed in this test run was <1s (controller registered well within the first poll); 5s is generous to absorb cold-cache JVM variance on slow CI hardware. **Critic findings + fixes applied:** **TECHNICAL NOTABLE #1 (applied):** missing field-count assertion. Original draft only spot-checked individual fields, would silently swallow a wire-shape regression (added field without schema bump). Fix: added `assertEquals(4, myEntry.size(), ...)` mirroring the WebTable size-assert convention; cites the exact field list in the failure message so a future maintainer sees what changed. **TECHNICAL NIT (applied):** redundant `assertNotNull(gameId)` AFTER `asText()` (which never returns null — JsonNode coerces missing to `""`). Replaced with `assertNotNull(myEntry.get("gameId"))` to actually pin node-presence before the `isBlank()` check. **TECHNICAL NOTABLE #2 (NOT applied — out of scope):** test leaves the running game in the shared JVM `GameManager.getGameController()` map for the rest of the test class. The critic flagged this but acknowledged equivalence-to-status-quo: the existing `endToEnd_createTableAddAiJoinStart_advancesTableState` at line ~1098 does the same thing without cleanup, and the `DELETE /api/rooms/{roomId}/tables/{tableId}` route at `WebApiServer.java:721` is for WAITING tables (semantically unclear if it works mid-game without explicit concede). Per-test cleanup should be a separate slice that bundles BOTH game-starting tests + likely needs a concede or game-end RPC, not a one-off cleanup in this slice. Queued as a follow-up. **DEFERRED slices documented inline in the brainstorm:** REJOIN-C (most-recent-disconnect ordering) — single-game case is 99% of users; defer until multi-game rejoin shows up in the wild. REJOIN-D (rejoin-as-spectator for conceded games) — engine permits it but no user has asked; defer to avoid lobby clutter on a niche flow. Both deferrals are explicit user-confirmed scope choices (AskUserQuestion answered "Skip REJOIN-C" + "Skip REJOIN-D" at scope-lock time) — not silent drift. **Why not service-level unit test instead of HTTP integration test:** the user explicitly chose Full HTTP at scope-lock (AskUserQuestion answer). The HTTP shape exercises: (a) auth middleware → SessionEntry lookup → user UUID resolution; (b) the reflection-based GameLookup helpers under real concurrent engine state; (c) Jackson serialization of the DTO record; (d) Javalin route registration. Service-level unit would skip (a), (c), (d). The HTTP shape is the real customer of the surface (the live lobby polls HTTP, never the service directly), so it's the right contract to pin. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none — test-only file under `Mage.Server.WebApi/`. **File LOC discipline:** test files are exempt from the 500 LOC hard cap per CLAUDE.md; `WebApiServerTest.java` is now ~1490 LOC (was ~1411). **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — server-side integration test, no DOM / layout / engine / wire surface. **Spec rigor (ADR 0014):** D1 paraphrase block written in conversation pre-build with explicit AskUserQuestion confirmation of "Full HTTP integration test" interpretation before any code touched. D2 acceptance criteria all countable (new `@Test` method exists; method name matches `getGamesMine_userInLiveGame_returnsThatGame`; method passes `mvn -f Mage.Server.WebApi/pom.xml test -Dtest='WebApiServerTest#getGamesMine_userInLiveGame_returnsThatGame'`; full `WebApiServerTest` class 70/70 green including 4 rejoin tests + e2e adjacency; field-count assertion present + asserts == 4; opponent-count assertion present + asserts == 1; gameId/tableId distinctness assertion present). **Test gate:** new test 1/1 green in 24.50s isolation + 70/70 green in the full `WebApiServerTest` class in 32.71s; no other tests touched; no production-code change so no other modules need re-verification. |

| Slice UIFIX-4 — Backdrop click + global Esc cancel cast-related dialogs (2026-05-11) | Standard (new hook + cross-cutting Esc handler in GameDialog; per-method dispatch matrix; no wire/schema/DTO change; LOC discipline observed by extracting the hook to its own file when the inline version pushed GameDialog past the 400 soft cap) | Builder + Technical critic (single-pass; UI/UX SKIP — uses existing fuchsia-dialog visual language and the same Skip wire each dialog's × close button already dispatches; Magic-rules SKIP — no engine semantics changed; Graphical SKIP — no motion change). | **Trigger:** user-reported batch item #4 — clicking outside a cast/confirmation modal needs to auto-cancel the in-flight action so the user can try again without losing the phase. **Recon surprise:** TargetDialog / SelectDialog don't route through the centered-modal branch at all (gameTarget→DialogBanner / LibrarySearchModal; gameSelect with cardsView1→bottom-right floating). The scry/surveil case the user cares about most has NO scrim at all. So a centered-backdrop click alone wouldn't help. **Two-channel solution:** (a) centered-modal backdrop onClick wired for the dialogs that route there (gameChooseChoice, gameChoosePile, gameInformPersonal/gameError); (b) global document-level Esc keydown listener in GameDialog that fires the same dispatcher regardless of routing — Esc reaches the bottom-right scry/surveil modal where the backdrop can't. **Per-method dispatch:** gameTarget/gameSelect/gameChoosePile (allowSkip = !flag || min===0) → all-zeros UUID; gameChooseChoice (!flag) → empty-string; gameInformPersonal/gameError → close-only; gameAsk/gameSelectAmount/gameChooseAbility/gameSelectMultiAmount → null (no skip — user must answer). MulliganModal disables Esc intentionally and the `escEnabled` gate respects `isMulliganDialog` to preserve that load-bearing decision. **LibrarySearchModal / TriggerOrderDialog** already use `useModalA11y` capture-phase Esc with `stopImmediatePropagation`; our bubble-phase handler runs after them and gracefully no-ops when they consume the event first. **File LOC discipline:** the inline version pushed GameDialog.tsx from 348 → 453 LOC (past the 400 soft cap). Extracted `useDialogSkipDispatcher` to its own file (`webclient/src/game/dialogs/useDialogSkipDispatcher.ts`, ~60 LOC) to keep GameDialog at 392. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none — the existing per-dialog Skip wire is reused verbatim. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — dialog surface, not pod layout. **Test gate:** webclient typecheck clean; 4 new tests in `GameDialog.test.tsx > backdrop / Esc dismiss` cover dispatch (gameChooseChoice flag=false → empty-string), Esc-as-universal-escape, mandatory no-op (gameChooseChoice flag=true), gameAsk no-skip. Full suite 1767/1769 (+4 new; same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog failures). |
| Slice UIFIX-2 — Expose freeMulligans in Create Game form (2026-05-11) | Standard (webclient form change + WebApi integration test pinning the wire round-trip; no schema/DTO change — `freeMulligans` field already on the create-table DTO; no engine touch) | Builder + Technical critic (single-pass; UI/UX SKIP — single number input following the existing playerCount input pattern in PreLobbyModal; Magic-rules confirms the engine math is correct end-to-end per the recon agent's deep trace) | **Trigger:** user-reported batch item #2 — players still asked to discard a card on their first (free) mulligan even when host sets freeMulligans=2 in Edit Settings. **Recon depth (very long agent dispatch):** every code path on the PATCH side traces correctly: `EditSettingsModal.tsx:166-171` PATCH dispatch, `LobbyService.updateMatchOptions:868-875` applies `setFreeMulligans(v)`, `MatchImpl.getOptions()` returns the same reference Match construction captured, `TwoPlayerMatch.startGame:18 / CommanderDuelMatch.startGame:31 / etc.` read `options.getFreeMulligans()` AT startGame time (not constructor), `LondonMulligan.mulligan:80-117` has correct deduction=0 logic when `usedFreeMulligans.get(player) < freeMulligans`, and `LondonMulliganTest` passes for free-mulligan scenarios. This guarantees the engine sees the right value at startGame regardless of any downstream PATCH bug. **Implementation:** new `freeMulligans: number` state in `PreLobbyModal.tsx` (0..5 number input, clamped via existing clamp helper, default 0 preserves historical behavior); added to POST body. **Server-side regression test:** new `createTable_withFreeMulligans_persistsValueOnResponse` test in `WebApiServerTest.java` — POSTs `{freeMulligans:2}` in the create body, asserts the WebTable response's `freeMulligans` field is 2. TableMapper:144 reads `match.getOptions().getFreeMulligans()` directly, so a response value of 2 IS proof the engine will see 2 at startGame time. **What's NOT changed:** no engine code, no schema bump (`freeMulligans` was already on `WebCreateTableRequest`), no DTO change, no client-side wire schema change. **Open follow-up:** if the user still sees the bug post-merge, the failure is on the PATCH path specifically (smaller surface to investigate) and the create-time path provides a real user-facing workaround. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — lobby surface. **Test gate:** new WebApi integration test 1/1 green in 21.46s; webclient typecheck clean; webclient suite 1767/1769 (same 2 pre-existing baselines). |
| Slice UIFIX-1 — Retry table-subscribe gate to absorb seat-write race (2026-05-11) | Standard (single-file defensive WebApi change; retry loop around an existing gate; no wire/schema/DTO change; the race is timing-dependent and not unit-testable without WebSocket harness work the slice doesn't take on) | Builder ONLY | All critics skipped — `defensive retry-with-grace-window around an existing gate; no behavior change on the happy path or the genuine-denial path beyond a bounded 300ms latency budget on the bad cases. The intermittent failure mode has no reliable repro, so a critic pass can't easily catch a regression we don't have a test for either.` | **Trigger:** user-reported batch item #1 — intermittent "Connection rejected; subscribe denied caller is not seated" when joining a game from the lobby, no reliable repro. **Recon (long parallel Explore agent):** emit site is `TableStreamHandler.java:178` (`closeWith(ctx, 4003, "Subscribe denied: caller is not seated.")`). Gate: `publiclyVisible = !passworded && spectatorsAllowed; seated = isUserSeated(initial, session.username())`. Snapshot at WS-upgrade time via `currentSnapshot(tableId)` which reads upstream Table state via reflection. **Race hypothesis:** between the upstream Table seat-write completing (POST /seat returns 204) and our snapshot reading the seat via reflection, there's a small window where the seat list doesn't yet include the just-joined player. No event-ordering plumbing between the two layers. **Fix:** retry up to 3 times at 150ms each (300ms total bad-case latency) — happy path's first poll wins so common-case latency is unchanged; intermittent race absorbs the timing gap; genuine non-members still hit 4003 after the grace window. **Trade-offs:** spectator-on-private-table rejection now takes up to 305ms before close-frame (acceptable given the alternative is the user-visible "Connection rejected" papercut). **Why no test:** the race is timing-dependent and `TableStreamHandler` requires a WebSocket harness that the existing test suite doesn't have; building one is a separate slice. The change is defensive and the failure mode is intermittent; if the retry proves insufficient we can re-ladder from 3×150 to 5×100ms. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — WS handshake, not in-game layout. **Test gate:** WebApi test-compile green; existing `WebApiServerTest#createTable_*` and `#patchTable_*` cases all pass — no behavior change for the happy path. |

The webclient knew about its OWN mulligan via the open MulliganModal but had no opponent visibility — a long-standing wire gap lampshaded by a code comment in `MulliganModal.tsx:25-29`. **Wire change:** `WebPlayerView` gains `mulliganDecisionPending: boolean` field (now 30 fields, locked by `GameViewMapperTest#playerView_jsonShape_locksThirtyFields_schema136`). | Slice UIFIX-5 — Enable rollback-turns voting by default on new tables (2026-05-11) | Standard (single-line server-side default flip + regression test; vote-prompt forwarding and webclient UI deferred to a follow-up per user direction — the engine has the full feature wired but `USER_REQUEST_DIALOG` callback forwarding from upstream → our WebSocket is the gap, marked at GameDialog.tsx:36 as a known deferral) | Builder ONLY | All critics skipped — `single-line default flip + regression test; the deferred vote-prompt forwarding and webclient UI are tracked as a separate follow-up slice; no behavior change beyond "engine permits rollback requests on tables where the upstream code path was already wired"; full multi-critic pass will run on the follow-up that builds the vote dialog.` | **Trigger:** user-reported batch item #5 — native xmage's "Go back N turns" voting feature should work in the new web client. **Recon (long parallel Explore agent):** engine has the full feature — `PlayerAction.ROLLBACK_TURNS` (initiate), `ADD_PERMISSION_TO_ROLLBACK_TURN` / `DENY_PERMISSION_TO_ROLLBACK_TURN` (vote responses), `GameController.java:520-676` (server-side dispatch + vote collection), `GameSessionPlayer.java:133-158` (sends `UserRequestMessage` dialog to each non-requesting player), `GameImpl.rollbackTurns(int)` at `GameImpl.java:4060-4082` (executes restoration from saved state checkpoints). All 4 rollback actions already on `PlayerActionAllowList.java:51-74`. **The gap that silenced the feature:** `MatchOptions.rollbackTurnsAllowed` is a Java boolean (defaults to false), and `MatchOptionsBuilder` never explicitly enabled it. `TableController.java:674` copies match-level → per-game `GameOptions.rollbackTurnsAllowed` at startMatch, so every new table launched with rollback voting silently disabled at the engine level. **Fix:** explicit `options.setRollbackTurnsAllowed(true)` in `MatchOptionsBuilder.build()` after the other server-side default setters (quitRatio, minimumRating). Per user direction this slice — always-on now, host-toggle in EditSettings deferred until competitive play surfaces a need. **Test:** new `createTable_enablesRollbackTurnsByDefault` in `WebApiServerTest.java` POSTs a vanilla create-table body and asserts `match.getOptions().isRollbackTurnsAllowed() == true` via the TableController accessor (same pattern as the freeMulligans test from UIFIX-2). **DEFERRED to a follow-up slice (with explicit user confirmation at scope-lock):** USER_REQUEST_DIALOG callback forwarding by `GameStreamHandler`, new webclient dialog method to render the vote prompt, "Request Rollback" button + sub-modal in ActionPanel for picking turn-count, the ADD/DENY action dispatchers, and any schema change for the new dialog wire shape. Without those, a user CAN send `PlayerAction.ROLLBACK_TURNS` but other players never see the vote prompt — request times out. **Why ship the server-side enabler alone:** it's strictly additive (engine already supports the path it just wasn't enabled), independently testable, and unblocks the follow-up slice from having to also flip the default in the same commit. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none — this slice. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — server-side. **Test gate:** new test 1/1 green in 21.30s isolation; no other WebApi tests touched. |

Eye tracked the glowing parcels and lost the number. **Pivot at recon:** initial scope thought `parcelSchedule()` math was the bug; tracing the actual returned values showed 6-damage HEAVY already lands at 500ms, 1000ms, …, 3000ms (evenly spaced). The bug was visual feedback on the badge, NOT scheduling. Pivot saved test churn + reduced blast radius. **Real fix:** new `LifeBadge.tsx` with three motion layers: (a) per-tick scale-pop + brightness pulse via `key={tickKey}` remount, (b) sustained red text + heavier drop-shadow + `translateY(-8px)` lift while `pendingTicks > 0`, (c) outer scale to 1.8× during damage so the bloom is unmissable. | Slice WarmerDust — Bigger + warmer creature-death dust (2026-05-12) | Standard (3 webclient/src/animation files; visual tuning + small Preset-shape change adding `palette: readonly string[]` field; no wire/schema/DTO change) | Builder + planned LIVE feel-check via cinematic lab `triggerCreatureDies` | Technical critic SKIP — `pure preset-literal + constant tuning; ImpactParticles has no dedicated test file; CardAnimationLayer.test.tsx assertions are presence-only (tile-dust-overlay testid at :304/:314/:376), not duration-coupled; Preset shape change is backwards-compat (exile gets a single-entry palette so the type stays uniform)`. UI critic SKIP — `covered by live feel-check; user is the UX critic via lab's triggerCreatureDies`. UX critic SKIP — `no new interaction surface; tuning of an already-spec'd destroy event`. Magic-rules SKIP — `no engine semantics`. | **Trigger:** user follow-up to LifeBadge slice — destroy/disintegrate animation reads underwhelming on a crowded battlefield. **Recon found:** infrastructure already shipped (CardAnimationLayer + ImpactOverlay + ImpactParticles + CardDeathSequence + creature_died emission in gameDelta.ts:223), so this is tuning, not net-new. Bottleneck was the dust preset itself: 10 zinc-600 particles at 4px for 600ms over a neutral-zinc tile-block tint reads as "small dark fade" against busy permanents. Pivot mirrors the LifeBadge memo's pattern ([[feedback-bad-bug-hypothesis-pivot-to-visual]]) — the math + event wiring was already correct; the gap was visual saturation. **Tuning:** (a) `DUST_DURATION_MS` 600→1000ms so brightness-flash + crumble + drift have time to register; (b) `PRESETS.dust.count` 10→20 (2× particles); (c) `particleSizePx` 4→7 (~3× pixel area); (d) replace single `color: string` with `palette: readonly string[]` of 4 colors (zinc-600 + amber-900 + amber-700 + orange-600 ember); each particle picks one at random on mount via `palette[Math.floor(Math.random()*palette.length)]` so the burst is a mixed field, not monochrome dots; (e) widen downward drift from (±32, 24-56) to (±40, 32-80) so particles travel further over the longer duration (per-particle velocity roughly unchanged); (f) `ImpactOverlay.tsx:80` dust tile-block tint rgba(82,82,91,0.85) → rgba(120,53,15,0.8) (amber-900) so the keyframe's 0-8% brightness-flash pulls warmth out (reads "burned + crumbled" instead of "faded gray"). Exile path untouched (single-entry palette for backwards-compat). **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** T1/T2 unaffected (overlay is `position: fixed` at tile bbox via ImpactOverlay, not a grid-cell participant); T3 unaffected (particles render over card art, not on the card body); T4 (1440p — particle drift maxes at 80px down + 40px lateral, well-contained at a typical card-tile footprint); T5 (no engine/wire/schema bump); T6 (tabletop is production default — destroy ships to the right surface); T7 (4-pod arrangement unaffected). **Perf:** 20×4 = 80 GPU-transform divs peak in a 4-tile board wipe; well under the MAX_CONCURRENT_DISINTEGRATES=4 envelope and trivial for the GPU. **Test gate:** webclient typecheck clean; full suite 1773/1775 (same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog failures from before this slice); CardAnimationLayer dust-overlay presence assertions still green. **Deferred:** stack→battlefield landing flourish for non-cinematic-tier permanents (next slice; user picked destroy first). |
| Slice ShatterDeath — Replace dust visual with card-art shatter on creature death (2026-05-12) | Standard (6 files; new ShatterOverlay component + ImpactOverlay branching + CardAnimationLayer imageUrl wiring + CSS keyframe + sourceResolvers helper + test testid rename) | Builder + **explicit parallel 3-critic pass per user direction** (CSS / animation / GUI) BEFORE deploy to lab | All three specialist critics ran in parallel — `user explicitly asked for "CSS expert, animation expert and GUI expert agents to review this before deploying to Cinematic Lab as well." Each got the same file list (ShatterOverlay.tsx, ImpactOverlay.tsx, CardAnimationLayer.tsx, sourceResolvers.ts, index.css, CardAnimationLayer.test.tsx) with their own lens.` Technical critic skipped (the CSS + GUI critics covered the technical surface — perf, types, race conditions, file-size discipline, z-index, hooks rules). Magic-rules critic skipped — `no engine semantics; pure visual replacement of a previously-shipped destroy animation`. | **Trigger:** previous warmer-dust slice was still too small to read on a crowded 1440p tabletop (user feedback: "dust is tiny on-screen, I cannot tell where it flies"). User asked for alternative suggestions; picked "shatter into shards" from a 4-option menu (flash+bloom / diagonal dissolve / shatter / sink+crack). **Recon:** the lab itself was missing DeltaPump + CardAnimationLayer mounts (a separate fix shipped immediately before this slice as `fix(cinematic-lab): mount DeltaPump + CardAnimationLayer`, commit `407c67a5b1`), so events were never reaching ImpactOverlay in the first place — explains why the user couldn't see the warmer dust either. With the lab pipeline restored, the dust-vs-shatter visual comparison is now actually possible. **Implementation:** new `ShatterOverlay.tsx` (~170 LOC final) renders 8 triangular wedges via clip-path polygons all meeting at card center (50%,50%); each wedge fills the card via `inset:0` so percentage clip-path coords adapt to any aspect ratio; each shard paints the dying tile's actual `<img>.src` as background-image (resolved at event-handler time via new `resolveTileImageUrl` helper, before React reranders the tile out of the DOM); single CSS keyframe drives outward translate + rotate + scale + brightness-flash, parameterized via per-shard CSS vars (`--shard-dx`, `--shard-dy`, `--shard-rotate`). ImpactOverlay's `kind='dust'` branch now delegates to ShatterOverlay (unconditional useEffect short-circuits to preserve rules-of-hooks). CardAnimationLayer extends `ActiveImpact` with `imageUrl: string \| null`. Testid renamed `tile-dust-overlay` → `tile-shatter-overlay` (3 sites in CardAnimationLayer.test.tsx; no other consumers in tree). **Critic findings + fixes applied pre-commit:** Animation [BLOCKER] easing curve was a soft `cubic-bezier(0.22, 0.6, 0.36, 1)` (glide feel) — swapped to front-loaded `cubic-bezier(0.1, 0.7, 0.2, 1)` so shards JUMP then decelerate. Animation [NOTABLE] flight distance marginal at 1440p — `SHARD_FLIGHT_FACTOR` 1.0 → 1.5 (shards clear neighbor-card buffer). Animation [NOTABLE] brightness flash 1.6× too subtle — bumped to 2.2× peak at 12% (120ms) to match exile path's punch and let the eye register as a beat. CSS [NOTABLE] missing `will-change` — added `will-change: transform, opacity` (8 shards × 60fps × 1000ms compositing without the hint can jank on weak GPUs). CSS [NOTABLE] implicit transform-origin fragility — added explicit `transform-origin: 50% 50%`. CSS [NOTABLE] reduced-motion JSDoc mismatch — corrected to describe actual behavior (shards mount at initial state, AnimatePresence exit conveys death signal independently). GUI [NOTABLE] face-down card edge case + z-index ladder relationship — documented both in JSDoc. **Critic findings overruled (with rationale):** CSS [BLOCKER] "polygons fail on non-square cards" — checked the math: percentages adapt to any aspect ratio (8 triangles = 2-per-quadrant via diagonals from center to each corner), the polygons tile any rectangle. CSS [BLOCKER] "transform composition order makes shards tumble wrong" — current `translate(dx,dy) rotate(r) scale(s)` composition makes each shard pivot at its apex (card center) by `r` then translate outward; this is the designed shatter feel (wedges fanning + sliding), not the "shard tumbling around its visible centroid" the critic expected. Marked as design choice, not bug. GUI [BLOCKER] "z-index fragility" — critic explicitly admitted "accidentally correct today"; downgraded to NOTABLE and addressed via JSDoc clarification. **Deferred follow-up:** CardAnimationLayer.tsx now at 607 LOC (already over the 500 hard cap pre-slice; this slice added ~15 LOC for imageUrl wiring). File has a documented exception in its module JSDoc. Technical critic skipped — `the CSS + GUI critics covered hooks-rules, race conditions, file-size discipline, type-safety, and the DOM-mutation race; magic-rules N/A`. Magic-rules critic skipped — `no engine semantics; pure visual layering of an overlay on top of existing resolve_to_board events`. | **Trigger:** user moved from destroy cinematic to "how cards make from stack to battlefield (the 'flying' effect)." Picked all-non-token permanents (coverage gate) + arc + scale-up (aesthetic) from 4-option menu. **Recon found:** existing path is just Framer's LAYOUT_GLIDE spring (~400ms straight-line interpolation) for all permanents, plus an EXTRA GroundCrackOverlay for cinematic-tier (commander / PW / CMC≥7). No "flight" feeling for routine resolves. **Implementation:** new `ResolveFlightOverlay.tsx` (~180 LOC final) renders a Framer `motion.div` with `x/y` keyframes sampled at 25 points along a quadratic bezier (source = stack focal center, target = destination tile's FINAL layout position via new `resolveTileLayoutBBox` which walks `offsetLeft/offsetTop` instead of gBCR to bypass Framer's mid-glide transform interpolation), `scale` keyframes `[1, 1.4, 1]` at `times [0, 0.5, 1]`, `filter` brightness `[1, 1.4, 1]` at same waypoints. Duration 700ms (`RESOLVE_FLIGHT_MS`). New `setTileOpacity(cardId, value)` helper mutates inline `style.opacity` on the destination tile during flight so Framer's parallel LAYOUT_GLIDE-driven copy of the same card is invisible (no double-image). CardAnimationLayer's `resolve_to_board` handler now does TWO things: (a) mount ResolveFlightOverlay for all non-token permanents (new), (b) ALSO mount GroundCrackOverlay for cinematic-tier (existing path retained). Flight at 0-700ms + ground crack starts at +400ms → reads as "card descends, ground cracks as it lands." Lab affordance: new `triggerResolvePermanent` + "Permanent resolves" button (11th lab button); bypasses gameDelta by directly emitting `resolve_to_board` via `playCardAnimation` since the lab is testing the overlay, not the diff path. **Critic findings + fixes applied pre-commit:** CSS [BLOCKER] `setTileOpacity(cardId, null)` cleared inline `style.opacity` to `''`, but React's next render would immediately overwrite to its prop value (`1`) BEFORE the 200ms transition completed — result was an instant pop, not a fade. Fixed by setting explicit `'1'` on restore (transition interpolates 0→1 visibly), then a deferred `setTimeout(250ms)` clears the inline rule once the visual end-state already matches React's natural value. Animation [NOTABLE] flat 120px arc disappeared on long diagonal flights at 1440p — changed to `Math.max(120, distance * 0.4)` so cross-pod resolves get a proportionally taller arc. GUI [NOTABLE] smoke test missing — added "click doesn't throw" smoke for the resolve-permanent button (full overlay-mount assertion needs jsdom layout metrics which are unreliable; smoke locks the wire path: button → trigger hook → playCardAnimation → handler entry). **Critic findings overruled with rationale:** none this slice — all blockers / notables addressed or explicitly deferred. **Deferred follow-up (CALLED OUT BY GUI CRITIC AS OVERDUE):** CardAnimationLayer.tsx is now ~700 LOC, 200+ past the 500 hard cap. The pre-slice exception is no longer sufficient — extraction into per-cinematic hooks (`useResolveFlightHandler` / `useImpactHandler` / `useCommanderReturnHandler` / `useCastHandler`) is the next slice's work. GUI critic recommended doing this BEFORE the next cinematic slice; user can choose when to gate. Also deferred per animation critic: bump scale `1.4→1.5/1.6×` or brightness `1.4→1.6×` for more spectacle; bezier samples `24→32-36` for smoother curves at high-refresh. These are tuning rounds queued for after the user's live A/B feedback. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none. **Upstream rebase impact:** none. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** T1/T2 unaffected (overlay is `position: fixed`, not grid participant); T3 explicitly satisfied (overlay paints the dying tile's actual `<img>` src as background-image — full Scryfall art preserved); T4 (1440p — distance-scaled arc peak 0.4× distance keeps long flights cinematic; 25 bezier samples acceptable for 60fps); T5 (no engine/wire/schema bump); T6 (tabletop production default — resolve ships to right surface); T7 (4-pod unaffected). **Test gate:** typecheck clean; full suite 1774/1776 (1 new resolve-permanent smoke pass + same 2 pre-existing TargetDialog failures from previous baseline). **Live A/B status at commit time:** Vite HMR running on localhost:5174; user has not yet refreshed — parallel-critic pass requested as gate before lab verification. |

(b) Cmd+Enter inside NewDeckModal triggers form submit; (c) color-identity gate blocks off-color adds (red Lightning Bolt blocked from a mono-green commander deck with Yeva, Nature's Herald). **Implementation notes:** A1 fix uses a useEffect cleanup keyed on `deck.id` that captures the previous render's `deck.id` by closure and writes the latest `renameDraft` (via ref) to that deck — survives both the rail-switch race and the unmount-on-tab-leave case; A3 falls open when commander metadata is still loading OR when no commander is present (non-Commander format), so it never silently blocks legitimate adds; B11 sticky search uses `position:sticky top-0` inside the workbench's `overflow-y-auto` editor column — pins the search bar regardless of scroll depth without breaking standalone DeckEditor callers (sticky degrades to no-op when the parent has no scroll); fix-1's full-bleed routing splits App.tsx's tab content into two branches — `tab === 'decks'` gets a `h-screen flex flex-col overflow-hidden` outer container with the workbench in a `flex-1 min-h-0` wrapper, other tabs keep the legacy `min-h-screen` + `max-w-4xl mx-auto` shape. **DTO firewall:** unchanged. **Schema impact:** none — webclient only. **Upstream rebase impact:** none — webclient only. **Tabletop T1-T7 verified:** N/A — deck-builder lives outside the game window. **Test gate:** typecheck clean; ---

## Open questions (for future reference)

- **Should the Graphical specialist always run for slices that touch Framer / CSS animations, even if the slice is primarily structural?** Slice 70-E skipped Graphical and shipped a Tailwind grid-template-areas tokenizer bug; would Graphical have caught it? (Probably no — that's a UI/build-time concern. The skip was correct.)
- **Is "GUI" ever non-redundant with "UX + UI"?** ADR 0011 line 195 says yes for structural rewrites; cadence-reform tier table says "redundant with UX + UI" and drops it. Slice 70-D + 70-E both skipped GUI without missing anything. Worth confirming or flipping the convention after 3 more architectural-tier slices.
